## 1. Goal, scope, and non-goals

Deliver one trustworthy, versioned memory route. A permitted agent and user can search, page, inspect exact evidence, understand authority and freshness, correct or reject a claim, and see the effect across CLI, TUI, and dashboard. Archive, expiry, changed content, revoked grant, foreign owner, stale source, or unverifiable baseline cannot become current authoritative context through another read path. Project knowledge and consented personal preferences remain available across appropriate agents; task constraints stay task-bound. Optional hybrid retrieval earns use through a measured comparison; lexical retrieval remains functional without an embedding engine.

In scope: F01–F08, F15, F18–F19, F23–F24, A02–A03, B05, E06/E19/E25, R01–R03/R05–R06/R08/R18, AP01, AP07's canonical memory administration and user search, and this phase's AP08 acceptance. This includes ordinary retrieval, historical administration, relations and derived summaries, trust, maintenance, preference migration, permissions, explanation, and live human interfaces. The report's unnumbered authority/applicability/contrary-evidence/lifetime rules (§6.8), corrections (§6.12), subject coverage (§2), and current-versus-proposed documentation (§4.4) also bind this plan.

Other plans own host discovery and lifecycle, AST graph production and dynamic architecture/UI consumers, and token optimization. This plan supplies their safe read and correction substrate; it does not implement those outcomes. No new storage engine, daemon, unprompted Git hook, automatic host capture, inferred user approval, simulated metric, or product-wide readiness claim. No phase-wide dependency on Phase 70, Phase 71, or the other memory plans.

### 2.1 Fixed proposed interfaces and persistent state

All interfaces below are **proposed changes owned by these tasks**, not existing APIs. Reuse current store transactions, version history, relations, CAS receipts and Phase 70 strict-request validation. Do not add a framework or database.

1. **One defensive decision, T01.** `retrieval.py::VisibilityDecision` (WT `:259`, frozen) and `evaluate_artifact_visibility(store, artifact, *, access: MemoryAccess, mode: Literal["ordinary","inspect","historical"]="ordinary", now=None, memo=None)` (WT `:555`) exist at `fe1ffb0`. `access` is a `retrieval.MemoryAccess` (`:140`; kinds `policy`, `legacy_allowlist`; `legacy()`, `resolve()`, `recheck()`, `historical_allowed`) built from 73-T02 resolution, never an `OwnerScope` masquerading as permission; §2.2 D1 fixes when `legacy_allowlist` is permitted. Return `visible`, `usable_as_current`, `freshness` (`current`, `stale`, `unknown`, `not_applicable`), `reason_codes` and `reference`. Ordinary delivery requires both booleans true. A physically contained, exact source hash/revision and supported parser establish `current`; missing baseline/hash, unsupported parser, missing source or parse failure establish `unknown`, never current. Unanchored explicit preferences have `not_applicable` source freshness and remain usable only when their own authority, applicability, expiry and access pass. Freshness classification of every writer's rows per §2.2 D33 and PR1. Authorized inspection may show stale/unknown content with qualification; denied owner/source never exposes content, IDs, counts or existence. Source-dependent learned advice, cached memory, graph consumers, summaries and procedure applicability call the same decision before use.
2. **Identity and digest, T01/T02.** Reference contains exact `artifact_id`, `artifact_version`, canonical `project_id`, `worktree_id`, `owner_scope`, `source`, `subject`, trust/provenance and freshness. Preserve current `MemoryArtifact.content_hash` meaning: **source file hash**, not memory payload hash. Add `payload_hash` for SHA-256 of exact persisted `memory_artifact_versions.content.encode("utf-8")`; compute once on authorized writes and version it. Add nullable `payload_hash` to current/version rows and a write-authorized additive backfill. Read-only old schemas return `migration_required` with explicit upgrade action; no backfill, key creation, journal or migration on read. An unsafely unbound old version cannot become verified by hashing its currently tampered bytes. Reopening an old schema for historical inspection reports missing integrity evidence. Implemented at `fe1ffb0`: `payload_hash`/`payload_hash_origin` columns (`store.py:372-373`, `:596-597`), backfill `_migrate_payload_hash_columns` (`:678`) only from the writable constructor (`:1152`), `migration_required` from the read-only view (`:2059-2116`), integrity label set `{"verified","backfilled","unbound","mismatch"}` (`retrieval.py::_integrity`, WT `:374-406`); backfilled and unbound rows stay usable and labelled (owner decision OD-1, 2026-10-05); a STATED row is backfilled only when its signature matches (`store.py:661`); when new content parses equal to stored content `_write_version` (`store.py:768`) keeps the stored text verbatim and its binding via `_content_unchanged` (`:742`) and `_persisted_text` (`:759`); `RecursionError` (`store.py:721`) and BLOB content (`_stored_text`, `:733`) are handled.
verified-by: `rtk grep` of the cited lines at `fe1ffb0` plus `pytest tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py tests/test_memory_authorization.py -q -p no:cacheprovider` → 395 passed, 1 deselected (2026-10-06). `source_ref` retains path/symbol, revision, source digest, worktree dirty digest and parser coverage supplied by Phase 74; unsupported coverage remains unknown. No path or externally supplied digest establishes permission or truth.
3. **Strict requests, T06.** Extend Phase 70 `src/rush/mcp_support/request_models.py` operation variants (`_memory_tag:673`, `_memory_spec:682`, `memory_request_model_keys:723`, `MEMORY_FIELDS:548`), `tools/memory.py::MemoryOperation` (`:83-106`) and `VALID_OPERATIONS` (`:109-132`), dispatch and `tool_registry.py` registration together. With `schema_version:2` the nested `request.limit`/`request.max_bytes`/`request.max_tokens` govern; a top-level `limit` or `max_bytes` (`_MEMORY_COMMON`, `request_models.py:521-522`) beside `schema_version:2` is `E_INPUT`. `request` has `schema_version:2`; reject unknown keys, boolean-as-integer, omitted/null mismatches and simultaneous legacy + strict fields before effects. Read common fields: `subjects` (nonempty subset of the seven existing subjects), `query` (default empty for list), `owner_scope`, `sources` (nonempty subset of resolved grant), `trust_tiers`, `freshness`, `archive_mode` (`active` default or explicitly authorized `historical`), `limit` (1–100, default 20), `max_tokens` (omitted → existing `DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS=2048`), `max_bytes` (omitted → existing `DEFAULT_MAX_BYTES=8192`), `cursor`. Both budgets are strict Python/JSON integers ≥1 (boolean, float, string, null, zero and negative rejected `E_INPUT` before effects); no new upper cap is introduced because current request validators have none. Positive integers are type-valid, then admission preflight computes minimum bytes and tokens for a complete canonical `E_BUDGET`/error envelope plus transport framing through the existing serializer and token counter, before extracting content or opening a writable store. If either cap is below that computed minimum (including `max_bytes=1` or `max_tokens=1`), reject `E_INPUT` before effects: its bounded protocol-validation error is counted separately as admission failure, never labeled in-budget or context-success. Admitted requests guarantee their complete success or `E_BUDGET` response fits both caps, including envelope/cursor/framing; no hidden extra allowance. If the required-size diagnostics themselves would exceed either admitted cap, emit only the complete canonical minimum error fields permitted by the serializer, with no content, cursor or false completion; preserve detailed required-size diagnostics only when they fit. `limit` is strict integer 1–100 (omitted → 20; explicit null invalid). Omitted `owner_scope` resolves only the current canonical project's authorized owner from the resolved policy; when multiple owner grants could match, return `E_INPUT` requiring explicit owner instead of combining them. Explicit owner null is invalid. Omitted `sources` uses exactly that resolved grant's approved source set; empty/null/non-string entries are invalid, supplied sources must be a nonempty subset, and no grant means denied rather than an ambient wildcard. `cursor` omitted/null starts first page; supplied cursor must be nonempty string. Legacy adapters preserve prior omitted/null semantics and cannot broaden a v2 request. `history` requires `artifact_id` plus historical permission; `explain` requires exactly one of `artifact_id` or nonempty `query`. Explain returns `supporting`, `contrary`, `attempted`, `affected` evidence refs and `unknowns`; it performs no model call. Results retain ToolResult and existing fields, add schema version/refs/exclusion reasons and continuation, and retain existing error codes `E_OWNER`, `E_SCOPE`, `E_VERSION`, `E_RESTART`, `E_BUDGET`, `E_NOT_VISIBLE`, `E_PERMISSION`, `E_MIGRATION`, `E_OFFSET`, `E_INPUT`, `E_UNAVAILABLE` (`tools/memory.py:159` `_CODE_STATUS`). Error mapping: missing/invalid policy, no grant, revoked, expired, relinked, moved root, or operation not granted → `E_PERMISSION` with `reason` equal to the `resolve_memory_scope` reason; explicit owner not granted → `E_OWNER`; requested sources not a subset of the grant → `E_SCOPE`; owner omitted with more than one granted owner → `E_INPUT` (`field="owner_scope"`). No new error code is introduced.
4. **Mutation transaction: correction core 72-T05; v2 dispatch, receipts, event index and import seam 72-T06.** 72-T05 owns the correction core: versioned `applicability` column/versions, task binding, ordinary `usable_as_current` False for `rejected`/`superseded`/`pending`/task mismatch (a row with non-NULL `task_id` is usable as current only when `(task_project_id, task_worktree_id, task_id)` equals `(access.project_id, access.worktree_id, access.task_id)`, all non-`None`; omitted task context never matches; a `legacy_allowlist` access never matches except preference `resolve`, which in `legacy` mode matches the caller-supplied `(project_id, worktree_id, task_id)` (PR3)), current task context `MemoryAccess.task_id: str | None = None` (new; `MemoryAccess.legacy`, `MemoryAccess.resolve` and `retrieval.read_access` gain keyword `task_id=None`; `recheck()` passes `task_id=self.task_id`; narrows only, never passed to `resolve_memory_scope`) from optional top-level request `task_id` (`_MEMORY_COMMON`, `NonEmptyStr | None`; `MemoryTool.__call__`/`run` gain `task_id: str | None = None`) threaded into every `MemoryAccess` a `MemoryTool` operation builds (reads and mutations; it changes only `usable_as_current`: `write`/`edit`/`archive`/`delete`/`correct` checks and results are unchanged by it, while `handoff` selection and `sender_task_id` follow it (D21)); TUI and dashboard pass `None`; CLI `rush memory ask`/`recall`/`list` (`cli.py:3441-3532`) and every `_build_memory_operation_command` leaf (`cli.py:3796`) gain `--task-id ID`, passed as that top-level `task_id` only when given; handoff `prepare` persists `access.task_id` as `sender_task_id` (D21) and `receive`, its `ack` and the D22 bridge resolve with `task_id=sender_task_id`; `retrieval._cursor_binding` adds `task_id`, the correction write in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` under the policy lock, the `supersedes` edge, new `relations.insert_relation_tx(conn: sqlite3.Connection, *, source_id: str, source_version: int, target_id: str, target_version: int, kind: str, origin_ref: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]` (callers: correction core, `add_relation`). It runs inside the caller's open `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and never begins or commits. It checks: `kind ∈ RELATION_KINDS` (`relations.py:44`); both versions pass `sqlite_integer_in_range` (`store.py:65`); for a kind in `_ACYCLIC_KINDS` (`relations.py:61`), no self-loop and no `_supersedes_cycle_exists` (`relations.py:96`); each `(id, version)` exists in `memory_artifact_versions` with `deleted = 0`. Then it inserts. It returns `add_relation`'s dict codes (`OK` + `sequence`; `E_INPUT`; `E_NOT_VISIBLE` for a missing endpoint version) and does no visibility, owner or current-version check. `add_relation` keeps its signature, its precondition checks, and (inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`) its visibility/owner/current-version checks, then calls it. Before its own writes, the correction core checks that every cited `supersedes` entry is the current version and `visible` under `access` (ordinary mode; `usable_as_current` is not required, so `pending`, stale-anchored and task-bound rows stay correctable), with the row owner equal to `access.owner_scope` and `store.project_root == access.storage_root`. It then writes `supersedes` edges from replacement@1 to each cited version; an edge stays valid after its target version becomes non-current. The existing legacy receipt is written through `receipt_writer: Callable[[sqlite3.Connection, dict[str, Any]], None] | None`, called once after every other effect and before commit with `{artifact_id, artifact_version, result_refs: [{artifact_id, artifact_version}], result}`. 72-T05 binds `store._write_receipt(conn, receipt_operation_id, artifact_id, "edit", artifact_version, result)` when a `receipt_operation_id` is given, else `None`. 72-T06 binds `store._write_v2_receipt(conn, invocation_id=…, effect_id=…, request_digest=…, artifact_id=…, kind="edit", revision=artifact_version, payload={binding, result_refs, result})` (D29) in that same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. Extend current `edit`, `archive`, `delete`, `promote`, `link`, `maintain` variants rather than add browser-specific operations. All previews are read-only; apply requires `apply:true`, explicit execution permissions, current resolved policy, exact owner and current expected revisions. `archive` with `archived:false` is restore. Correction uses `edit` with `correction:{kind:"correct"|"reject"|"supersede", reason, supersedes:[{artifact_id,artifact_version}]}`; rejection appends a version with non-actionable applicability, supersession creates current replacement plus version-bound `supersedes` edge, preserving originals. 72-T05 adds nullable `applicability` (`pending`, `active`, `rejected`, `superseded`; NULL = no applicability gate), `task_project_id`, `task_worktree_id`, `task_id` to `memory_artifacts` and to `memory_artifact_versions`, plus `binding_recorded INTEGER` on `memory_artifact_versions` (1 = snapshot written; NULL = pre-T05 version); additive `ALTER`s in the constructor post-script transaction and in `upgrade`; `MemoryArtifact` (`store.py:327`) and `_INSERT_SQL` (`:625`) gain the four fields. All three version insert sites write them in the same transaction: `_insert_row` (`:2457`) from the artifact, `_write_version` (`:826`) copies the current row's values unless the caller passes `binding=` (correction core passes it and updates `memory_artifacts` to the same values), `_backfill_legacy_row` (`:2281`) writes NULL with `binding_recorded` NULL. Columns are authoritative; preference content `applicability`/ids (item 5) are written equal in the same write. D38 `task_binding` = `(task_project_id, task_worktree_id, task_id)`. One `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` validates all versions, owner and scope, writes replacement/history/relations/receipt and commits atomically; any failure rolls back all effects. Acquire the existing policy CAS/physical-root serialization boundary through commit, so revoke and apply cannot interleave unchecked; lock order per the §2.2 Lock order table. Generation advances on content/visibility changes; dependent summaries, embeddings, cached guidance and continuations are invalidated by exact ref/revision on their next read, not merely UI refresh. Receipt identity `(invocation_id,effect_id)` is persistent; same identity + same request digest returns original result, different digest conflicts (`E_VERSION`, `reason="effect_digest_conflict"`, zero effects), independent same-content writes remain distinct; storage, migration and legacy-receipt compatibility per §2.2 D29. Event identity `(project_id, worktree_id, task_id, event_id)` named by Phase 73 line 201 per §2.2 D30: same payload returns the existing reference, changed payload returns `E_VERSION` `reason="event_digest_conflict"`, never a second row. On lock timeout or conflict return no effects, retaining user form/preview for fresh review; never blind retry with broader grant.
5. **UTF-8 and preferences, T02/T05.** Expansion selects `representation:"base64"|"utf8"` (base64 default for legacy calls). Offset/next offset are **byte offsets into exact persisted UTF-8 JSON**, never character indexes. UTF-8 mode returns `content_utf8`, `payload_hash`, artifact/version, total bytes and next offset; reject non-boundary/negative/out-of-range offsets (`E_OFFSET`), never clamp. Integrity labels and BLOB/non-UTF-8 handling per §2.2 D32. Each slice is scalar-safe; combining marks may span slices but concatenation reconstructs exact original bytes. Apply the same preflight admission minimum before expansion; a cap below that minimum is `E_INPUT`, not an oversized purportedly in-budget `E_BUDGET`. Test both caps at 1, each computed minimum−1, each exact minimum and one-scalar required size: no extraction or mutation before admission, exact canonical serialized/error and transport byte/token measurements, no successful-context receipt for admission failures, and admitted error output within both caps. If full envelope plus one scalar cannot fit, return `E_BUDGET`, no cursor/false completion; provide required minimum byte/token envelope, without bypassing caps. Preference content schema keeps `{key,value}` compatibility and adds `scope` (`personal`, `project`, `task`), `authority` (`explicit`, `inferred`, `imported_unverified`; the last is written only by import and stays as provenance; an imported record applies only when `applicability="active"`, set by import-time `confirm_constraint_ids` or later local review; an imported `preference` record additionally needs `promote_stored_artifact(user_stated=True)` (D28)), `applicability`, task/project/worktree IDs, `expires_at`, source and correction refs. Same-scope explicit conflicting records remain unresolved unless a user-reviewed supersession edge establishes order; timestamps alone cannot manufacture approval. Task end excludes task record only. Applicability match keys per §2.2 D28. Personal overlay stays a distinct consented store root, using exactly the same schema.

**Implementation prerequisite route:** owner decision 2026-10-05: Phase 72 branches from Phase 70 tip `07775f1` and Phase 70 stays unmerged; implementation continues on `phase/72-memory-correctness-and-user-control` in `/Users/jamesdsizemore/Developer/rush-cli-worktrees/phase-72` using its one existing `.venv`. Commit each task when its §5.0 gates pass; never start a task on top of an uncommitted earlier task. Merge, push or PR only on a separate owner go, except the 72-T09 native-lane draft PR (§2.2 D17). Stage T01 pure defensive logic/T04 import fix independently; final grant acceptance consumes only 73-T02's policy component. Pure parser/visibility tests may run without hosts; user-visible/transport claims require their real routes. Do not substitute mock grants for final acceptance.

Authority policy: current host instruction hierarchy and current explicit user direction govern; Rush memory never manufactures instructions or grants. Within authorized memory at a task: explicit task constraint > explicit project decision/preference > consented explicit personal preference > inferred project/agent pattern. Later explicit correction supersedes earlier same-scope content, with history retained; conflicting same-priority claims are shown unresolved until user/evidence resolves them. An inference cannot promote itself to a user rule. Trust tier, source integrity, owner permission and applicability are independent; passing one never bypasses another. Personal overlay is off until consented; project overrides do not rewrite personal originals. A task record is bound to project/worktree/task identity and is not inherited by unrelated tasks. Historical/admin reads are explicit, labeled, permissioned and excluded from ordinary agent recall.


### 2.2 Binding decisions

Owner decisions are dated; rows marked *plan resolution* settle a case no owner decision covers, choosing the option that keeps full scope.

| D1 | **Policy governs all routes** (owner decision 2026-10-06). New `retrieval.memory_policy_mode(project_root, *, data_root=None) -> Literal["policy","legacy","unavailable"]`, decided by the **D1 mode predicate** table below. In `policy` mode every route in §2.3 resolves through `MemoryAccess.resolve`; a `session_allowlist` only narrows `requested_sources` and never constructs `MemoryAccess.legacy`; a legacy-allowlist or no-owner call that cannot resolve returns `E_PERMISSION`. In `unavailable` mode every route returns `E_PERMISSION` (`reason` from the predicate table) with zero effects. In `legacy` mode existing behavior is unchanged except D3. Race rule: legacy `MemoryAccess.recheck()` (today returns `"ok"` unconditionally, `retrieval.py:230-231`) returns `denied` when `memory_policy_mode(project_root, data_root=access.data_root) != "legacy"` or the canonical root changed, so `MemoryAccess.legacy` (`retrieval.py:164-181`) gains `data_root: Path \| None = None`; every read route calls `_recheck_code` after selecting rows and before building the response; every mutation (legacy or policy) holds `hold_policy_lock(data_root=access.data_root)` → re-evaluates mode and access → `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` → effects → `retrieval.recheck_failure(access)` (new: `None` iff `access.recheck() == "ok"`; `changed` → `("E_RESTART","access_changed")`; `denied` → `("E_PERMISSION", reason)`, reason = `resolve_memory_scope(...)["reason"]` re-run with the access inputs for policy access, `"policy_mode_changed"` for legacy access) → non-`None` → rollback, that code and reason, no receipt → else commit. Policy lock: `hold_policy_lock` calls `cross_process_project_lock(lock_id, data_root=root, storage="directory")`; `cross_process_project_lock` and `_acquire_cross_process_project_lock` (`dashboard/state.py:64`, `:110`) gain `storage: Literal["file","directory"] = "file"`; `"directory"` on POSIX opens the data root with `os.open` flags `O_RDONLY` and `O_DIRECTORY`, requires `os.fstat(fd).st_uid == os.geteuid()` (else `OSError`), then `fcntl.flock` with `LOCK_EX` and `LOCK_NB` retried every 0.02 s up to 30 s (still busy → `OSError`); `OSError` → mutations `E_PERMISSION` `reason="lock_unavailable"`, grant writers `E_LOCK` (`_locked`, `authorization.py:771-779`), zero effects; a data root this call creates uses `mkdir(mode=0o700)`; no file is created; Windows named mutex per §5.0 G3; the shared thread-local `held` counter (`_cross_process_lock_depth`, `dashboard/state.py:60`) keeps nesting on one fd. A mutation proceeds without the lock only when the data root is absent AND that `mkdir` raises `OSError` AND `load_policy_readonly(data_root)` returns `missing`. *Plan resolution:* a revoked or expired grant, a moved/relinked/copied root and an unreadable registry never select `legacy`, so revocation never reopens legacy access. D1 registration marker: empty `<canonical project root>/.rush/memory-policy.required`, written only by `grant_memory_scope` (72-T01 R21), never removed by revoke, and kept out of version control: after `_check_version` and before the pending marker is created, again immediately after it is created and before `_commit` (and on every identical-grant return, marker present or not, before any final-marker write) `grant_memory_scope` creates `<canonical project root>/.rush/.gitignore` containing `*` (the existing Rush convention, `core/git_sandbox.py:51-53` and `patch/sandbox.py:27-31`, which write it only when absent), or, when the file exists and its last line that is neither blank nor `#`-prefixed is neither `*` nor `memory-policy.required*`, appends `memory-policy.required*` (preceded by `\n` when the file is non-empty and does not end in `\n`; both sandbox dirty-workspace filters, `core/git_sandbox.py:39-43` and `patch/sandbox.py:44-49`, also skip a porcelain line whose path is `.rush/.gitignore`) (flags and failure rules in 72-T01 R21; a `.gitignore` failure never blocks or fails the grant; a failed new grant undoes its own `.gitignore` steps (72-T01 R21) before its `rmdir` of `.rush`; a failed identical-grant marker write keeps them), and the repository `.gitignore` gains `.rush/memory-policy.required*` (72-T01), so a clone of a granted project carries no marker and a no-policy checkout stays `legacy`; "D1 marker present" has one definition, D1 predicate row 2 (the final marker or a `memory-policy.required.pending-*` entry), used by rows 2, 2a, 7 and 7a; a missing policy or another data root's policy never selects `legacy` for a marked project. *Marker population:* no marker-less grant exists outside test/temp data roots, so nothing writes markers for earlier grants: the only grant writer is commit `15c4fe7` (73-T02/POLICY), contained only in branch `phase/72-memory-correctness-and-user-control`, in no tag and not an ancestor of `main`, and the default data root policy file (`POLICY_RELATIVE_PATH`, `authorization.py:51`) does not exist (verified-by: `rtk git branch -a --contains 15c4fe7` → only `phase/72-memory-correctness-and-user-control`; `rtk git tag --contains 15c4fe7` → none; `rtk git merge-base --is-ancestor 15c4fe7 main` → exit 1; `~/Library/Application Support/Rush/user/.rush/memory_authorization.json` absent; `rtk grep -rn grant_memory_scope src/` → only `authorization.py` (no CLI/MCP caller at `fe1ffb0`); `rtk git branch -r --contains 15c4fe7` → none; 2026-10-07); 72-T01 R21 adds the marker writer on that branch and the §5.0 merge gate keeps the branch off `main` until R21 is committed, so every grant a shipped Rush writes carries a marker. |

Expiry is decided in Python by `evaluate_artifact_visibility`; `recall_page` never passes `now=` to `search_candidates` (an SQL expiry prefilter under `LIMIT/OFFSET` loses rows between pages). `_list_filtered` applies the same Python decision. |

| D8 | Corroboration (72-T04): a peer counts iff same `owner_scope` and `subject`, `symbol_ref IS candidate.symbol_ref` (NULL-equal, as `maintenance.py:408`), `trust_tier != 'STATED'`; equal stored content (canonical `json.dumps(sanitize_value(content), sort_keys=True)`, `safety/redactor.py:52`); `evaluate_artifact_visibility` returns `visible` and `usable_as_current` (archive, D5 expiry); payload integrity not `mismatch`; `source` ∈ the resolved grant's `approved_sources`, ∉ `INTERNAL_MEMORY_SOURCES` (`store.py:50-56`), and differs from the candidate's and from every counted peer's; when both origins are non-null, `(origin_kind, origin_id)` differs (PR5: a null-origin peer counts in both modes when its source differs). Count = distinct `source` over {candidate} ∪ counted peers; promote iff count ≥ 2; the count is persisted in `PromotionResult.corroboration_count` (`trust.py:36-40`) and the row's `corroboration_count` column. An eligible peer with different content blocks promotion with new `PromotionDenialReason` `"contradicted"` (`trust.py:25`) only when `candidate.symbol_ref IS NOT NULL`; an unanchored (`symbol_ref IS NULL`) different-content peer neither counts nor contradicts; `INTERNAL_MEMORY_SOURCES` rows never count or contradict. Maintenance and public `promote` share one stored-evidence function. Raw `candidate_sources` without `candidate_refs` → `E_INPUT` on MCP (`request_models.py:567`), CLI (`cli.py:3608-3647`) and in-process `store.promote` (`store.py:2829`); CLI gains `--candidate-ref ID@VERSION@SOURCE` and the dashboard `memory_promote` form field `candidate_refs` takes the same items comma-separated, each parsed with `split("@", 2)` into the MCP ref shape `{"id", "version": int, "source"}` (`request.candidate_refs`, validated at `tools/memory.py:2047-2070`); `--candidate-source` alone → `E_INPUT`; dashboard `candidate_sources` without `candidate_refs` → `_ActionDenied(400, "malformed_request", "candidate_sources requires candidate_refs")` before `MemoryTool.run` (pattern `dashboard/server.py:2789-2791`). At `fe1ffb0`, `store.promote(id, user_stated=False, candidate_sources=["invented1","invented2"])` promotes to STATED with count 2. Replaces the `_candidate_sources` pool (`maintenance.py:405-413`); `trust.count_corroboration` (`trust.py:144-160`) is kept as the counter. Signature: `promote_stored_artifact(conn, artifact_id, *, store: TypedArtifactStore, access: MemoryAccess, user_stated: bool, candidate_refs: list[dict] \| None = None, expected_version: int \| None = None)` (`project_root` removed; `store.project_root` used). Target row read on `conn`; `evaluate_artifact_visibility(store, row, access=access)` not `visible` → `VersionConflictError(..., code="E_NOT_VISIBLE")`. `user_stated=True` → no peer scan, `corroboration_count 0` (D10; PR11). Else new `store.corroborating_peers(conn, store, candidate_row, *, access) -> tuple[list[MemoryArtifact], bool]` selects on `conn` every row meeting the D8 peer predicate with `source IN access.sources`, independent of `candidate_refs`; returns counted peers and `contradicted` (an eligible different-content peer exists); `contradicted` → denial `"contradicted"`; else pool = `[candidate.source] + [p.source for p in peers]` → `evaluate_promotion(candidate_sources=pool)`; `trust.count_corroboration` stays the distinct-source counter (D8 replaces the `_candidate_sources` pool, not the counter). Each `candidate_ref` must equal a counted peer `(id, artifact_version, source)`: absent or invisible id → `E_NOT_VISIBLE`; visible but stale or not a counted peer → `E_VERSION`; refs never remove a contrary peer. `store.promote` gains `access: MemoryAccess \| None = None` and passes it; with `access=None` it holds `hold_policy_lock()`, evaluates D1 mode: `policy`/`unavailable` → `MemoryPolicyError("E_PERMISSION", "policy_grant_required")` / predicate reason, zero effects; `legacy` → `MemoryAccess.legacy(root, <distinct DB sources> − INTERNAL_MEMORY_SOURCES, owner_scope=owner_scope or legacy_owner_scope(root), operation="write")`. `_run_promote` passes its resolved {write} access and maps `VersionConflictError.code` (not the literal `E_VERSION`, `tools/memory.py:2170-2181`). Maintenance `_mutate_promotion_sweep`/`_mutate_skill_admission_check` pass the cycle `store` and `access`, drop `_candidate_sources`, and persist `decision.corroboration_count` (replaces `count_corroboration` at `maintenance.py:447`). |

the runner applies the same replacement to each element of a decision's `verify_attempt` `request.contract.required_commands[*].command` before the call, and an `episode` decision request shows the case `inputs.task`, `permitted_actions`, `editable_paths`, `acceptance.behavior_ids` and `acceptance.required_commands` verbatim (`{python}` unexpanded); the report records `sys.executable` and `sys.version`. `receiver_readback_acknowledged`: the runner binds refs from this episode's `write` events with `status == "ok"` whose `raw.subject`/`raw.source` equal `acceptance.handoff.subject`/`.source`, count equal to `acceptance.handoff.write_count`; per bound `(raw.id, raw.artifact_version)` it reads `TypedArtifactStore.open_readonly_view(root)` → `get_version_payload(id, version)` (`store.py:1495`) `(content, payload_hash)`, digest = sha256 of `content.encode("utf-8")` (the bytes `acknowledge_handoff`, `store.py:2719`, checks), requires `payload_hash` NULL or equal to digest, `normalized_contains(content, acceptance.handoff.content_contains)` and `get_current_row(id)["artifact_version"]` (`store.py:1486`) equal to the bound version; binds the handoff `session_id` from `raw.data.handoff_id` of this episode's single `handoff` event with `status == "ok"` (zero or several → the case fails) and passes only when `TypedArtifactStore.open_readonly_view(root)` → `get_handoff_receipts(session_id)` (`store.py:2708`) maps every bound `id` to its bound `version` (`acknowledge_handoff`, `store.py:2719`, verified the digest). Refs and digests come only from tool results and the store, never from decision output. `fixture_revision` (D13) is the lowercase hex sha256 of `compact.canonical_json` of `{posix relative path: lowercase hex sha256 of bytes}` over regular files under the case root once building/seeding completes and before the first decision call, excluding `.git/` and `.rush/`; the runner recomputes it and rejects before any call on mismatch or when the builder's returned task differs from `inputs.task`. Exact baseline case objects per the **D13a manifest baseline cases** table. On success: `os.fsync(fd)`, new `store._fsync_dir(project root)` (mirrors `project_run.py:1421`) when this call created `.rush`, `os.unlink(<temp name>, dir_fd=fd)`, `os.fsync(fd)`. Windows (`os.name == "nt"`) keeps path operations: the temp is created through `open_contained(<temp rel>)` with `O_CREAT \| O_EXCL`, written, flushed and closed, then linked by path, each step preceded by the `open_contained` revalidation, plus a post-link check that `os.stat(open_contained(".rush/memory.db"))` identity equals the temp's (mismatch → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="path_escape"`, no path-based cleanup); directory fsync is skipped and publication durability relies on NTFS metadata journaling (declared; the native Windows lane runs the crash test). After a successful link that passes the post-link check, `memory.db` is never unlinked: a directory-fsync failure → `E_UNAVAILABLE` `reason="publication_unconfirmed"`; a temp-unlink failure → success, orphan removed by later cleanup under the target lock; retry replays via `find_import_effect_readonly`. Orphan cleanup runs only under that lock and removes `memory.db.import-*` temps. `_note_inserted`/`note_committed_write` run only after successful publication; the temp-bound instance is discarded after publication (later reads construct a normal store). Replay inserts only keys with no `(origin_kind="preference", origin_id=key)` row; an existing origin row (value or `{"deleted":true}`) wins and is never rewritten by migration. | D38 | Explain evidence per the **D38 explain buckets** table: one hop; visible rows only; each bucket sorted by `(artifact_id, artifact_version)` and deduped within the bucket. |
| D39 | Hybrid cache fill and completeness. Fill needs {write} on `access.owner_scope` (policy mode) plus `cache_write`; otherwise embeddings are computed without persistence and hybrid reads through `open_readonly_view` (no `.rush` creation); `legacy` mode stays `cache_write`-only. `hybrid_candidates` embeds missing texts in slices of `embeddings.MAX_BATCH` (public rename of `_MAX_BATCH = 32`, `embeddings.py:19`), one `embed_fn` call per slice, and calls `_recheck_code(access, "E_PERMISSION")` (`retrieval.py:1124`) before each slice and before the query embedding; non-`None` → returns `{"available": False, "code": <code>}` and `hybrid_page` returns `_page(code)` with zero items, no lexical fallback, no persistence; the policy lock is never held across `embed_fn`: embed, then lock → resolve {write} → per row persist only if `source` ∈ write grant sources and `artifact_version` unchanged and `embedding_text_hash` = sha256 of `embedding_text(json.loads(_stored_text(content)))` recomputed from the current row equals the hash the vector was computed for (stored in `memory_embeddings.content_hash`, `store.py:542`; never compared with artifact `content_hash` or `payload_hash`) → `put_embedding(..., write_sources)` (`store.py:1873`) re-reads the row inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` and inserts only on all three; `embedding_text` moves to `store.py`, `retrieval.embedding_text` re-exports it → release. `complete=True` only when every eligible candidate is emitted and the pool is not capped; otherwise `complete=False` with `incomplete_reason ∈ {candidate_cap, item_limit, byte_budget, token_budget}`; no hybrid cursor; a fallback cursor is labelled lexical; pool 0 → `complete=True`, zero items. Before emission (persistence enabled or not): `_recheck_code(access, "E_PERMISSION")`; `store.current_generation()` (`store.py:1227`) equal to the value read before embedding; each selected row re-read by `get_current_row` (`store.py:1486`) with unchanged `artifact_version` and `evaluate_artifact_visibility(..., now=<fresh time.time()>, memo=SourceValidationMemo())` `usable_as_current`; any change → `E_RESTART`, revocation → `E_PERMISSION`, zero items. Each scan passes `scan_limit + 1` and processes the first `scan_limit` rows; `candidate_cap` iff either scan returned more than `scan_limit` rows (replaces `>=` at `retrieval.py:1984-1986`). |

**Lock order** (X1; identical for every mutation, D4 writer, maintenance batch and import):

| Position | Lock | Id string | `data_root` | Taken by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | dashboard project lock, only when the caller already holds it | project id via `mutation_lock` (`dashboard/state.py:281`) / `cross_process_project_lock(project_id)` (`:64`, used at `:2271`) | caller's | dashboard dispatch; `build_handoff` (under `server._dispatch_handoff_send:2457-2505`); exposure-reducing steps (D4) |
| 2 | policy lock `hold_policy_lock(data_root=access.data_root)` | `"memory-policy:" + <policy path>` (`authorization.py:222-230`); storage `storage="directory"` per the Policy-lock storage rule; never `dashboard/project_locks` | writer root | every mutation; D4 writers; maintenance apply; D24; D39 fill; `grant_memory_scope`/`revoke_memory_scope` |
| 3 | mesh lease (maintenance only) | existing `MeshLockManager` lease (`mcp_mesh/lock_manager.py:29`) | — | `run_maintenance_cycle` |
| 4 | at most one of `"plugin-trust:" + <ledger path>` (D26), `"preferences:" + <preferences.json path>` (D27), `"memory-capsule-target:" + <canonical target path>` (D18; DB path for import, `--output` path for export, `--instructions` path for bridge; Phase 73 plan line 459 now states this identical id and position) | as named | `None` | D26, D27, D18 |
| 5 | SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` | — | — | every write transaction |

| Rule | Value |
|---|---|
| Project lock | never acquired while holding the policy lock |
| Exposure-reducing cleanup | `mark_artifact_orphaned`, `revoke_handoff_session` take no policy lock |
| Network calls | the policy lock is never held across `embed_fn` |
| Reentrancy | policy lock reentrant only within one thread and one `data_root` (`dashboard/state.py:60-107`) |
| Policy-lock storage | Policy lock: `hold_policy_lock` calls `cross_process_project_lock(lock_id, data_root=root, storage="directory")`; `cross_process_project_lock` and `_acquire_cross_process_project_lock` (`dashboard/state.py:64`, `:110`) gain `storage: Literal["file","directory"] = "file"`; `"directory"` on POSIX opens the data root with `os.open` flags `O_RDONLY` and `O_DIRECTORY`, requires `os.fstat(fd).st_uid == os.geteuid()` (else `OSError`), then `fcntl.flock` with `LOCK_EX` and `LOCK_NB` retried every 0.02 s up to 30 s (still busy → `OSError`); `OSError` → mutations `E_PERMISSION` `reason="lock_unavailable"`, grant writers `E_LOCK` (`_locked`, `authorization.py:771-779`), zero effects; a data root this call creates uses `mkdir(mode=0o700)`; no file is created; Windows named mutex per §5.0 G3; the shared thread-local `held` counter (`_cross_process_lock_depth`, `dashboard/state.py:60`) keeps nesting on one fd. A read-only data root still locks; never under `<data_root>/dashboard/project_locks` |
| Capsule actions | never acquire the dashboard project lock; when dispatched under it, it stays outermost (position 1) |
| Residual cost | policy lock is per OS-user data root, so D4 writers and maintenance batches serialize across projects; accepted; G11 records suite wall time per round |

**D10 preference API** (`src/rush/memory/preference_store.py`):

| Member | Signature or rule |
|---|---|
| constructor | `PreferenceStore(project_root=None, *, data_root=None)`; creates nothing; `CASMapTransaction` built lazily (its ctor `mkdir`s the root, `transactions.py:73-76`) |
| `resolve` | `resolve(key=None, *, access, overlay_access=None, query=None, project_id, worktree_id, task_id=None, now=None)`; `project_id`/`worktree_id` must equal the access ids in `policy` mode else `E_INPUT`; required in `legacy` mode; in `policy` mode `task_id` must equal `access.task_id` else `E_INPUT`; never calls `get`/`list_all` |
| `record` | `record(key, value, *, access, apply=False, cache_write=False, scope, authority, task_id=None, expires_at=None, user_stated=False, attribution=None)` |
| `correct` | `correct(artifact_id, *, access, apply=False, cache_write=False, layer: Literal["project","personal"], kind, reason, expected_version, value=_MISSING, user_stated=False)`; `layer` must match `access.owner_scope` else `E_OWNER`; `personal` needs overlay-owner {write} (D2); `kind` `correct`/`supersede` with `value` writes explicit authority (STATED via `promote_stored_artifact(user_stated=True)`, D10) as a second version in the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` with `applicability="active"`, and every `correct` with `apply=True` (any `kind`, with or without `value`) needs `user_stated=True` per D10 (row "explicit authority or any `correct` apply without `user_stated=True`") |
| `end_task` | `end_task(*, access, apply=False, cache_write=False, project_id, worktree_id, task_id, expected_versions)` |
| `migrate` | `migrate(*, access, apply=False, cache_write=False, preview_digest)`; protocol D27 |
| `PreferenceError` | `PreferenceError(code, reason)`; `code ∈ {E_INPUT, E_PERMISSION, E_OWNER, E_SCOPE, E_VERSION, E_MIGRATION, E_UNAVAILABLE}` |
| destination | from `access` only; `apply=False` is a read-only preview with zero effects |
| missing `cache_write` | `apply=True` only: `E_PERMISSION` `reason="cache_write_required"` |
| explicit authority or any `correct` apply without `user_stated=True` | `apply=True` only: `E_PERMISSION` `reason="user_stated_required"` |
| previews | `apply=False` needs only the D2 preview set; never needs `cache_write`, `user_stated` or a write grant |
| `legacy` mode (D1) | `record`, `correct`, `end_task`, `migrate` (preview and apply) → `PreferenceError("E_PERMISSION", "policy_grant_required")`, zero effects (D10 owner decision: a preference write needs a policy `write` grant; PR12); `resolve` unchanged (caller `project_id`/`worktree_id` required, PR3); `record` has no id parameters |
| `get`, `list_all` | unchanged (owner decision); declared legacy compatibility readers outside MemoryTool routes; a guard test asserts no `src/` caller; known kept behavior: they call writable `read_origin` (creates `.rush/`) and return archived migrated rows |
| `get_preference_readonly` | today creates the root via `CASMapTransaction` (`preference_store.py:97-110`, `transactions.py:75`); gains a non-creating read in `preference_store.py` |
| transports | MCP, CLI (`rush memory preference`, registered in the `cli.py:3863` operation loop) and in-process return identical results (parity test) |
| `set`, `delete` | `policy` mode: `hold_policy_lock` (Lock order 2) → resolve {write} with owner `legacy_owner_scope(root)` and `requested_sources=["migration:preference_store"]` (the origin rows' writer source, `migration.py:312`) → `"preferences:"` lock (Lock order 4) → resume pending publications → snapshot `s = tx.read(allow_missing=True)`, `desired` = `s.data` with the key set/removed; `desired == s.data` and no change to the origin → return as today; no origin row `(origin_kind="preference", origin_id=key)` → `tx.update` only (as today); origin row present → one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`: new `migration.replace_origin_content_tx(conn, origin_kind, origin_id, content, *, created_at=None) -> int \| None` (the body of `replace_origin_content` minus connect/commit/`note_committed_write`; `replace_origin_content` keeps its signature and wraps it), then, only when it returned an int, `store._write_receipt(conn, "preference-publication:" + hashlib.sha256(json.dumps([key, action, desired, s.content_hash], sort_keys=True).encode("utf-8")).hexdigest(), <origin id>, "preference_publication", <new version>, {key, action, old_version: s.version, old_digest: s.content_hash, desired, stage: "committed"})` → commit → `note_committed_write` → `tx.write(desired, expected_version=s.version)` → `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, rewrite the receipt with `stage:"published"`, commit; a `None` return (origin content already equals `desired`) → rollback, then `tx.write(desired, expected_version=s.version)` only, no receipt. Failure before commit → rollback, JSON untouched, the exception propagates, zero effects. CAS conflict or write failure after commit → `PreferenceError("E_UNAVAILABLE", "publication_unconfirmed")`, JSON bytes unchanged, origin version and the `stage:"committed"` receipt kept; never reported as success or as zero effects. Resume (start of every `policy`-mode `set`/`delete` and preference `migrate` apply): for each `mutation_receipts` row with `operation_id LIKE 'preference-publication:%'` and `stage == "committed"`, in `created_at` order: live `version == old_version` and `content_hash == old_digest` → `tx.write(desired, expected_version=live.version)`, then `stage:"published"`; live `data == desired` → `stage:"published"` only; otherwise → `stage:"superseded"`, return `PreferenceError("E_VERSION", "migration_conflict")` with no JSON write (the key then appears in the D27 preview `conflicts`). denied → `PreferenceError("E_PERMISSION", reason)`, zero effects (no JSON write); `unavailable` mode → `PreferenceError("E_PERMISSION", reason)`; `legacy` mode unchanged; `replace_origin_content` keeps clearing `expires_at`/`expired_at` (`migration.py:133`) |
| `persona` | `cli.py::context_persona_cmd:5562`: `PreferenceStore().set` (`:5567`) raising `PreferenceError` → echo `skipped: <code> <reason>`, exit 0 (`_STATUS_EXIT_CODES["skipped"]`, `cli_support/rendering.py:54`), zero effects |

**D14a pairing and aggregation:** rows pair by `(provider, case_id, seed, fixture_revision, grants, budgets)` across current/hybrid/metadata; a missing pair → `unavailable`. Recall uses the frozen relevant-ID set over returned refs; zero-relevant cases count only toward unsafe/false inclusion. Per case, seeds are averaged first; `paired_bootstrap_ci` (`reporting.py:409`) runs over per-case differences (unit = case, seed = first `--seeds` label). The table below is evaluated per provider by new `reporting.aggregate_hybrid_verdict(rows) -> {"per_provider": {...}, "global": ...}`; global = `unsafe` if any provider unsafe, else `unavailable` if any, else `harm` if any, else `benefit` only if every provider is `benefit`, else `no_benefit`.

**D14a verdict rule** (evaluated in order; first match wins):

| Order | Verdict | Condition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `unsafe` | any unauthorized or stale inclusion in any arm |
| 2 | `unavailable` | any case's hybrid arm fell back or its route was unavailable |
| 3 | `harm` | literal recall mean difference (hybrid − current) < 0, or paraphrase paired-bootstrap `ci.high` < 0, or `hybrid_passed < current_passed`, or PR4 |
| 4 | `benefit` | paraphrase paired-bootstrap `ci.low` > 0 |
| 5 | `no_benefit` | otherwise |

**D13a manifest baseline cases** (all `variant` `base`, `retrieval_required` `false`, `budget` `{max_total_tokens:16000, output_reserve_tokens:1024, max_steps:12, timeout_s:600}`):

| id | kind / family | inputs | fixture_revision | grants | permitted_actions | editable_paths | acceptance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

**D38 explain buckets** (relation kinds `relations.py:44-53`):

| Bucket | Members |
|---|---|
| `supporting` | targets plus outgoing `validated_by`, `implements`, `depends_on`, `caused_by`, `fixed_by` edges |
| `contrary` | `contradicts` in either direction; `supersedes` edges targeting the target; visible rows with the same `(owner, subject, symbol_ref)` where `trust.evaluate_conflict(row, target) == "delete"` |
| `attempted` | visible failure/episodic rows linked by any edge; query mode also matches them |
| `affected` | relations touching the target, any kind, either direction; summaries whose `member_versions` contain the target; a shared 72-T05 `task_binding` alone adds no row |
| `unknowns` | `no_recorded_rationale`, `freshness_unknown`, `no_matches` |

### 2.4 Runnable baseline falsification and repaired-route check

Run this exact bounded probe at the Phase 72 worktree root. It creates only a temporary Git project, store, data root and policy (`HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME`, `LOCALAPPDATA` redirected before import). Observation at `fe1ffb0`+WT, 2026-10-06: `{"archive_hidden": true, "expansion_denied": true, "outside_denied": true, "paging": true, "ungranted_source_denied": false}` — `ungranted_source_denied` is false because a non-empty allowlist builds `MemoryAccess.legacy` and returns `x0` despite the registered grant (defect A1, 72-T01 R1). After 72-T01 R1 every field must be true.

```sh
cd /Users/jamesdsizemore/Developer/rush-cli-worktrees/phase-72 && rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python - <<'PY'
import hashlib, json, os, subprocess, tempfile, time
from pathlib import Path
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as d:
    home = Path(d) / "home"
    home.mkdir()
    os.environ["HOME"] = str(home)
    os.environ["XDG_DATA_HOME"] = str(home / "data")
    os.environ["LOCALAPPDATA"] = str(home / "data")
    from rush.memory.authorization import grant_memory_scope
    from rush.memory.retrieval import recall_page, expand_artifact
    from rush.memory.store import MemoryArtifact, TypedArtifactStore, legacy_owner_scope
    from rush.memory.trust import resolve_symbol_ref
    from rush.workflows.projects import register_project
    root = Path(d) / "project"
    root.mkdir()
    outside = Path(d) / "outside.py"
    outside.write_text("def Known():\n    return 1\n", encoding="utf-8")
    source = root / "source.py"
    source.write_text(outside.read_text(encoding="utf-8"), encoding="utf-8")
    for args in (["init", "--initial-branch=main"], ["add", "source.py"],
                 ["-c", "user.name=fixture", "-c", "user.email=fixture@example.invalid",
                  "commit", "-m", "fixture"]):
        subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=root, check=True,
                       stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    store = TypedArtifactStore(root)
    owner = legacy_owner_scope(root)
    for i in range(3):
        store.write(MemoryArtifact(
            id=f"p{i}", family="memory", subject="domain_knowledge", trust_tier="DERIVED",
            content={"text": "needle"}, source="allowed", created_at=time.time(),
            symbol_ref="source.py::Known",
            content_hash=hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), owner_scope=owner))
    store.write(MemoryArtifact(
        id="x0", family="memory", subject="domain_knowledge", trust_tier="DERIVED",
        content={"text": "needle"}, source="ungranted", created_at=time.time(),
        symbol_ref="source.py::Known",
        content_hash=hashlib.sha256(source.read_bytes()).hexdigest(), owner_scope=owner))
    register_project(root)
    grant_memory_scope(project_root=root, owner_scope=owner, approved_sources=["allowed"],
                       allowed_operations=["ask", "expand", "list", "write", "history"],
                       expected_policy_revision=0, consent=True)
    page = recall_page(store, subject="domain_knowledge", query="needle",
                       session_allowlist=["allowed"], limit=2)
    paging_ok = (page["complete"] is False and bool(page["next_cursor"]))
    store.archive("p2", expected_version=1, scope="domain_knowledge", owner_scope=owner,
                  apply=True)
    active = recall_page(store, subject="domain_knowledge", query="needle",
                         session_allowlist=["allowed"], limit=10)
    ungranted = recall_page(store, subject="domain_knowledge", query="needle",
                            session_allowlist=["ungranted"], limit=10)
    archived = store.get_current("p2")
    expansion = expand_artifact(store, artifact_id="p2", version=archived.artifact_version,
                                session_allowlist=["allowed"])
    results = {
        "paging": paging_ok,
        "archive_hidden": [item["id"] for item in active["items"]] == ["p0", "p1"],
        "expansion_denied": expansion["code"] == "E_NOT_VISIBLE",
        "outside_denied": resolve_symbol_ref(str(outside) + "::Known", root) is False,
        "ungranted_source_denied": ungranted["items"] == [],
    }
    print(json.dumps(results, sort_keys=True))
    assert results == dict.fromkeys(results, True), results
PY
```

Expected repaired output: `{"archive_hidden": true, "expansion_denied": true, "outside_denied": true, "paging": true, "ungranted_source_denied": true}`. 72-T01 R1 adds this probe as `tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py::test_t01_baseline_probe_policy_route`. Every T01/T02 test must retain owner/source policy, exact source freshness and tamper cases in addition to this minimum check.

## 3. Dependencies, file map, and ownership

`73-T02/POLICY` first implements/independently verifies the authorization producer. `72-T01` defensive predicates can start immediately; final grant-integrated acceptance consumes that checkpoint, not completed 73-T02 or host bootstrap/adapters. `72-T01` → `72-T02` then gates Phase 73's `73-T02/BOOTSTRAP-RUNTIME` automatic content delivery (stays Phase 73); `73-T02/BOOTSTRAP-CONSENT` (D16) runs after CODEC and before `72-T07` (§5.0); Phase 73 plan lines 117 and 499 name both checkpoints. Both checkpoints retain original 73-T02 identity and its entire outcome; this is an acyclic producer/consumer order. `72-T01` safe read/authority then gates any new automatic delivery. `72-T02` follows T01 for consistent page visibility. `72-T03` follows T01. `72-T04` can repair import independently; its promotion acceptance follows T01's authority rule. `72-T05` follows T01 for project-local work and the same narrow `73-T02` policy functions plus separately consented user-overlay grant for cross-project reads; it has no host-adapter dependency. `72-T06` follows T01–T05 for operations it exposes; a verified subset may integrate before optional hybrid. `72-T07` follows T06's exact operations, not completed Phase 70/71. `72-T08` follows T01–T02 and the `75-T02/EVALUATOR` checkpoint defined by D13, before 75-T02 whole evaluation completion. `72-T09` follows only implemented routes and exact live lanes it claims. `74-T01` supplies qualified symbols only where this phase displays code-linked references; actual-use labels use the existing Phase 70 T19 receipt readers (`read_artifact_receipts_readonly`, `telemetry.read_artifact_expansion_receipts_readonly`), and `75-T01` extends them when it lands. Portable-route closure (§8) also transitively needs Phase 73 73-T09 release prerequisites (75-T01 receipts, 75-T03/T04 budget and recovery); until they exist the §8 live lane stays open with that exact blocker. Until those producers exist, this phase displays `unavailable` with reason and does not fabricate values. No whole-plan wait.

### 72-T01 — Safe read, authority and symbol containment

Implement `72-T01 safe reads, authority and symbol containment` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: Current authorized evidence through every read path

Current authorized evidence through every read path.


#### Required behavior

 Implement one canonical-policy-plus-store eligibility decision for ordinary agent reads: canonical project/worktree, local grant and owner scope, explicit source allowlist, non-archived, non-expired, integrity-valid, current version, source-current or explicitly source-independent `not_applicable`, and applicable trust. Call `authorization.load_policy_readonly`/`resolve_memory_scope` against schema-1 policy before access and again on every page, direct expansion and mutation; compare monotonic `policy_revision`, canonical root/worktree, approved owner/sources/operations and revocation. A non-secret binding references policy but never grants access by itself. No policy/missing-store read creates files. A host/session string or supplied `OwnerScope` cannot grant access. Legacy explicit allowlist calls keep their established permission path only in D1 `legacy` mode; in `policy` mode an allowlist only narrows the resolved grant, and in `unavailable` mode every route returns `E_PERMISSION`; restricted receiver still requires its capability. Thread resolved owner/source scope from `MemoryTool` to store and retrieval; `legacy_owner_scope` handles only historical null owner rows, not a new caller's missing authorization. Apply before ranking and on each `recall_page`, `hybrid_page`, direct `expand_artifact`, and defended legacy recall. `E_NOT_VISIBLE` has indistinguishable missing/denied bodies. Both `TypedArtifactStore.edit` and legacy `update_content` atomically drop prior `STATED` authority/signature unless re-promoted from new evidence. Existing immutable history survives; only explicit historical admin mode can read excluded versions and labels why ordinary use rejects them. `resolve_symbol_ref` rejects absolute, `..`, and in-root symlink to outside before AST parse. `ApiDiffer` unknown baseline and missing/unparseable source cannot establish current evidence; distinguish unknown from stale, retain both in results.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/memory/store.py::search_candidates`, `scope_artifacts`, `recall`, `edit`, `update_content`, `_write_version`, `open_readonly_view` and shared eligibility helper; `src/rush/memory/retrieval.py::recall_page`, `hybrid_page`, `expand_artifact` and freshness helper; `src/rush/tools/memory.py::MemoryTool.run`, `_query`, `_compact_query` owner/source threading; `tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py::test_t01_denied_current_routes_and_authority`, `::test_t01_symbol_containment_and_unknown_freshness` (`:592`, kept as a T01 check). `trust.py::resolve_symbol_ref` containment and `tools/api_diff.py::ApiDiffer.diff_symbol` unknown baseline are done in `ef24aeb`; their single-snapshot read (R21) is a T01 deliverable. Import and consume `73-T02`'s `src/rush/memory/authorization.py::load_policy_readonly`/`resolve_memory_scope` without editing its policy file, grant/revoke writers or module, except the D1 registration marker and its `.rush/.gitignore` writer in `grant_memory_scope`. Preserve public envelope and legacy owner mapping. Do not implement a second validator in each UI.

#### Constraints

No new grant writer, duplicated policy, unsafe path parse, read constructor or implicit historical access; preserve Phase 70 demotion and all existing grant paths.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** T01 unit logic may precede policy integration; final policy/revocation checks require only 73-T02 authorization APIs. Extend `evaluate_artifact_visibility`/`VisibilityDecision` exactly as §2.1; `defended_recall`, legacy `recall`, compact/hybrid/expand, review citations and `token_economy/memory_cache_gate.py::check_memory_before_pack` must consume it. Add `src/rush/tools/review.py` and `src/rush/token_economy/memory_cache_gate.py` only for read-only store/access threading; reuse Phase 70 `open_readonly_view`, do not reimplement it.

#### Checks to run before reporting

All of these must pass: the task's tests pass with zero skips; the commands in this section, then §5.0 G11, where the full suite fails only on the two G16 task-start baseline tests.

**Concrete fixture and exact acceptance:** Seed `a1` active, `a2` archived, `a3` expired, `b1` foreign-owner, `u1` unknown source baseline, `t1` altered signed payload. Under Alice/source-a policy ordinary IDs are exactly `[a1]`; expansion of each excluded ID is `E_NOT_VISIBLE`. Authorized historical inspection may expose `a2/a3/u1/t1` only with exact reasons, never current authority. Missing DB stays absent; old schema returns migration-required without key/journal/DDL. Pause before response, revoke policy, then release: no payload leaves old scope. Test `review` citations and cache selection, not only low-level helper.

**RED → GREEN:** Create two owners/two source scopes, archived and expired records, edited promoted and physically tampered content, bad signature, unavailable Git baseline, missing content hash, symlink/absolute/traversal references. Assert exact row IDs never enter ordinary/compact/hybrid result or expansion, old authority is gone after edit, historical inspection marks exclusion, unknown remains unknown, and denied response leaks no ID/existence. Use 73-T02/POLICY's proposed exact fixture call `grant_memory_scope(project_root=root, owner_scope=owner, approved_sources=["source-a"], allowed_operations=["write","ask","expand","list","bootstrap"], expected_policy_revision=0, consent=True, data_root=tmp_path/"user-data")`; call `resolve_memory_scope(project_root=root, operation="ask", owner_scope=owner, requested_sources=["source-a"], data_root=tmp_path/"user-data")`. Resolved fields are `access` (`read`, `write`, `denied`), `reason`, `grant_id`, `policy_revision`, canonical `project_id/worktree_id`, owner, approved sources and operations; rejected binding has no usable grant. §5.0 G16.

### 72-T02 — Honest pagination and exact UTF-8 recovery

Implement `72-T02 exact-once paging and UTF-8 recovery` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: Every eligible record and exact byte sequence remains recoverable

Every eligible record and exact byte sequence remains recoverable.


#### Required behavior

 `recall_page` cursor records first unconsumed candidate when item count, token or byte limit stops output; `complete` means no eligible candidate remains, not merely scan cap exhausted. Bind cursor to project, worktree, subject/query, allowed sources/owner, ranking version and store generation; changed visibility/generation returns restart instead of duplicate/missed/foreign rows. Preserve bounded scan and truthful truncation. Add versioned `utf8` expansion mode returning complete Unicode scalar slices, artifact ID/version, offset/next offset, stored-content SHA-256, length and completion; reassemble exact original bytes and verify hash. Keep existing `content_base64` mode explicitly selectable for current callers; do not silently change its shape. `E_BUDGET` leaves no misleading complete page. Every page rechecks T01 eligibility.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/memory/retrieval.py::recall_page`, `_encode_cursor`, `_decode_cursor`, `expand_artifact`; `src/rush/tools/memory.py::_compact_query` request/response validation; `src/rush/memory/transport.py` only receiver decode/verification for the new mode, coordinated with 73-T05/T09 under C09; `tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py::test_t02_exact_once_paging_and_utf8_recovery`; `tests/test_memory_handoff.py::test_t02_receiver_digest_reconstruction`. Preserve base64 callers and token/byte caps.

#### Constraints

Preserve legacy base64 shape and byte-offset semantics; no skipped oversized candidate, clamped offset, false completion, scope restart hidden as empty, or read-side key creation.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow T01. Cursor includes resolved `policy_revision`, project/worktree, owner/sources/subjects/all filters, ranking and store generation; expiry eligibility time is rechecked on each response. Hold stable store snapshot for one page, revalidate revision/generation before emitting; mismatch returns `E_RESTART` without items. Advance scan offset only for candidates consumed, including rejected corrupt rows; first eligible oversized item returns `E_BUDGET` with same resume position and required minimum, so retry with adequate approved budget recovers it. Never emit repeating zero-progress OK pages. Add `representation`/`payload_hash`/boundary validation exactly §2.1.

#### Checks to run before reporting

All of these must pass: the task's tests pass with zero skips; the commands in this section, then §5.0 G11, where the full suite fails only on the two G16 task-start baseline tests.

**Concrete fixture and exact acceptance:** Seed IDs `p1,p2,p3` all containing `needle`. `limit=2` returns p1/p2 in deterministic rank/ID order with `complete:false`, next page p3 then `complete:true`; concatenated IDs equal all 3 with no repeats. Seed 513 matches and two interleaved corrupt rows; all 513 recovered exactly once. Stored JSON text contains `🙂é𐍈`; every allowed byte offset yields exact UTF-8 reconstruction and SHA-256; offsets inside scalar fail `E_OFFSET`, digest alteration fails receiver validation. Grant revocation/expiry or any filter/source/generation drift refuses continuation. Envelope-plus-scalar shortage yields no-progress `E_BUDGET`, later adequate budget retrieves same record.

**RED → GREEN:** Three matches at `limit=2`; >512 candidates; one record larger than budget; changed grant/source/store between pages; malformed/foreign cursor; emoji, combining mark and four-byte characters at every page boundary; altered page digest and stale version. Assert exact-once IDs, truthful next cursor/complete, bounded output, exact bytes/hash, denial after revocation and receiver rejection of altered content. Run `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py -k t02 -q` and `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_memory_handoff.py tests/test_memory_retrieval.py -q`.

#### Completion

 Every eligible match can be recovered once, exact artifact bytes reconstruct in both declared modes, and only listed files change for T02.

### 72-T03 — Relations and derived-memory validity

Implement `72-T03 authorized relations and current derived summaries` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: A derived claim cannot outlive visible supporting evidence

A derived claim cannot outlive visible supporting evidence.


#### Required behavior

 `add_relation` checks both endpoint versions, same canonical namespace, owner/scope authorization and T01 eligibility under an explicit grant inside the existing transaction; preserve cycle prevention. `related_artifacts` checks root and each hop immediately before return, including archive, expiry, integrity, freshness, version, owner and source. An invisible neighbor exposes neither ID nor count. `summary_is_current` and actionable retrieval require every member's current visible exact version; change, archive, expiry or revocation invalidates or qualifies derived summary before use. Contradictions, counterexamples and originals persist in history; a summary cannot increase member authority.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/memory/relations.py::add_relation`, `related_artifacts`; `src/rush/memory/consolidation.py::summary_is_current`; `src/rush/memory/retrieval.py` derived-summary eligibility adapter; `src/rush/tools/memory.py` relation-operation grant threading; `tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py::test_t03_owner_safe_edges_and_summary_invalidation`; `tests/test_memory_relations.py`, `tests/test_memory_consolidation.py` only if existing assumptions must change. No new graph store.

#### Constraints

No extra graph/database, foreign edge leak or authority increase. Root and every hop share T01 eligibility; historical traversal never supplies actionable context.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow T01. Extend `add_relation`/`related_artifacts` and `summary_is_current` with resolved `access`; canonical public `link` passes it. Recheck policy under shared mutation lock and both endpoints under transaction. Empty/malformed `member_versions` cannot certify a derived summary as current. Bind summary member refs to exact payload/source versions; derived authority cannot exceed weakest supported member. Correction/rejection invalidates all dependent consumers on next read.

#### Checks to run before reporting

All of these must pass: the task's tests pass with zero skips; the commands in this section, then §5.0 G11, where the full suite fails only on the two G16 task-start baseline tests.

**Concrete fixture and exact acceptance:** Alice owns s1/m1, Bob owns m2. Alice linking s1→m2 returns `E_NOT_VISIBLE` and SQL edge count remains 0. s1→m1 succeeds once; inverse supersedes cycle returns existing `E_INPUT` and edge count remains 1. Archive/expire/revoke/tamper/edit m1: summary `member_versions={m1:1}` is unusable; ordinary recall omits s1 and relations omit m1 with no invisible count. Permissioned history retains original members plus contrary evidence.

**RED → GREEN:** Two owners each with same subject, foreign edge, cross-project ID, stale endpoint, cycle, revoked source, tampered/archived/expired neighbor, changed member and contradiction. Assert rejected insert writes zero edges, traversal leaks no excluded IDs, invalid summary cannot be ordinary evidence, original/contradiction still inspectable under historical permission. Run `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py -k t03 -q` and `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_memory_relations.py tests/test_memory_consolidation.py -q`.

#### Completion

 Edge insertion and traversal obey same eligibility as recall, current summary cannot outlive eligible evidence, and only listed files change for T03.

### 72-T04 — Maintenance import and owner-isolated corroboration

Implement `72-T04 fresh maintenance imports and owner-scoped corroboration` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: Maintenance imports independently and changes only authorized evidence

Maintenance imports independently and changes only authorized evidence.


#### Required behavior

 Break `maintenance.py` import cycle within existing modules so fresh interpreter imports `run_maintenance_cycle` directly. `run_maintenance_cycle` takes a resolved `MemoryAccess` carrying the owner (D9); pass the caller's explicit source allowlist into candidate and corroboration queries, both using identical authorized owner/namespace and independent-source criteria. Never promote from agent/user name equality, duplicate source, foreign owner or a source denied by grant. Row failure remains isolated and reported; lock lease/loss semantics and expiry sweep remain intact. No maintenance read creates unrelated store.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/memory/maintenance.py::run_maintenance_cycle`, `_mutate_row`, `_SELECT_SQL` and import boundary; `src/rush/tools/memory.py::_run_maintain` to pass the permitted source allowlist; `src/rush/memory/store.py` only shared owner predicate required by maintenance; `tests/test_phase72_memory_correctness.py::test_t04_fresh_import_and_scoped_promotion`; `tests/test_phase62_maintenance.py` only to adjust existing contract. No alternate maintenance runner.

#### Constraints

No alternate runner, wildcard owner, foreign corroboration, source-label-only independence, swallowed lease loss or hidden preview writes.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Import regression can start independently. Preserve live Phase 70's import-order repair, which already passes fresh-process import; if integrating primary's older chain, define `MaintenanceTask` before plugin imports or use local `PluginTrustStore` import at `_mutate_skill_admission_check`. Do not add redundant refactor when existing repair is present. Preserve real Phase 70 preview/selected-ID/expected-revision/grant adapters. Candidate and `_candidate_sources` SQL bind exact owner/root/source grant plus active/current/payload checks. Independent source means distinct grounded source identity/provenance, not two agent labels or duplicate imported copies. Lock loss stops further rows and reports unprocessed IDs; already committed row receipts remain accurate. Locks follow the §2.2 Lock order table (policy → mesh lease → plugin-trust → SQLite).

All of these must pass: the task's tests pass with zero skips; the commands in this section, then §5.0 G11, where the full suite fails only on the two G16 task-start baseline tests.

Corrupt Alice row reports its exact ID, other valid rows preserve truthful counts; lease loss commits no later row. Preview leaves missing DB absent and apply expected-version drift changes 0 rows.
