### 72-T05 — Consented layered preferences and migration

Implement `72-T05 consented layered preferences and reversible migration` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: Correct guidance reaches authorized tasks without cross-project leakage

Correct guidance reaches authorized tasks without cross-project leakage.


#### Required behavior

  Keep project preferences in current project store. Personal overlay is opt-in under a **separate** `73-T02` local-user grant whose approved operations/sources expressly include overlay read for this canonical project/worktree; project-memory grant alone cannot expose it. Back overlay at literal `default_data_root()/"user"/".rush"/"memory.db"` through `TypedArtifactStore(default_data_root()/"user")`, reusing its schema and APIs, not introducing another memory implementation. `OwnerScope("user", str((default_data_root()/"user").resolve()))` labels rows but does not authorize reads; `authorization.resolve_memory_scope` rechecks user overlay grant/revision on each read and expansion. No global row is copied into project DBs. Only explicitly user-confirmed personal rules may enter overlay; project/task preferences never migrate upward automatically. Enforce instruction hierarchy and authority before scope: every inferred convention is advice only and cannot override any applicable explicit rule. Among applicable explicit rules, task > project > personal; same-scope conflict needs later user-reviewed supersession, otherwise remains unresolved and surfaced. Task constraint key includes project/worktree/task ID and expiry or explicit end. Agent host string is attribution, never authority. Existing `PreferenceStore` reads remain compatible; migration from `.rush/preferences.json` and `.migrated` is idempotent, compare-and-swap safe, and retains originals/backups until verified. Denied/disabled overlay yields project-only result, not leakage or a hidden write. Correction/rejection retains original, reason, source and affected downstream references.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/memory/preference_store.py::PreferenceStore` and scoped resolution; `src/rush/memory/migration.py::migrate_preference_store`; `src/rush/memory/store.py` only owner/version fields needed by existing schema; `src/rush/tools/memory.py` preference/correction operation; `tests/test_phase72_preferences.py::test_t05_scope_precedence_consent_and_migration`. Consume `73-T02`'s `authorization.py::resolve_memory_scope` and use `src/rush/setup/provision.py::default_data_root` unchanged; their source and consent writers belong to `73-T02`. No automatic personal import from native host memory.

#### Constraints

No overlay reads before separate consent, task leakage, inferred personal preference, upward migration or overwrite of user originals. Preserve PreferenceStore key/value compatibility.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow T01; consume only 73-T02 narrow policy component. Use §2.1 versioned preference schema and canonical correction transaction. Migration writes all selected rows in one authorized CAS transaction, checks count/content, then publishes the original per §2.2 D27 (`os.link` to `.migrated.<sha16>`, unlink source); failure leaves original usable, a differing existing backup is never overwritten. Record migration source digest/id and existing tombstones. Replaying same origin changes 0 rows and cannot resurrect deleted key. Read `PreferenceStore` without `_ensure_file` write on absent path; write initializer remains explicit.

#### Checks to run before reporting

All of these must pass: the task's tests pass with zero skips; the commands in this section, then §5.0 G11, where the full suite fails only on the two G16 task-start baseline tests.

**Concrete fixture and exact acceptance:** Personal explicit `format=compact`, ProjectA explicit `format=wide`, ProjectB no override, TaskA explicit `format=plain`: chosen values plain/wide/compact at applicable task/project scopes; ended TaskA returns wide. Personal explicit compact plus inferred project wide and inferred task plain still selects compact, with both inferences advice only; inferred wide also cannot replace explicit compact at equal scope. Alice correction reject/reversal is retained; held-out wording `verbose panels` retrieves applicable rejection while unrelated `wide integer` stays allowed. Disabled/revoked overlay yields project-only; foreign B private row never returned to A. Legacy `{format:compact}` replay inserts 1 then 0, deletion plus backup replay stays absent; concurrent edit returns exact `E_VERSION` and original stays intact.

**RED → GREEN:** Two projects/agents share consented personal rule but conflicting UI/project rules remain isolated; explicit user reversal; inferred repeated pattern; task end; declined consent; separate project-versus-overlay grant; revoked overlay policy revision; foreign host identity; legacy file and replayed `.migrated` backup; concurrent correction. Assert selected rule and provenance/why, zero foreign content, exact versions, no resurrection/duplication, and no file creation on read-only paths. Run `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_phase72_preferences.py tests/test_memory_compatibility_regressions.py tests/test_phase61_migration.py tests/test_phase41_memory_ship.py tests/test_phase53_state_writers.py tests/test_phase58_map_transactions.py tests/test_phase58_persistence.py -q`.

#### Completion

 Same-project agents resolve same permitted choices, authorized personal preferences cross projects without private leakage, migrations replay safely, and only listed files change for T05.

#### Corrections and test matrix (current HEAD `fe1ffb0`)

Facts: `PreferenceStore.__init__(project_root=None)` (`preference_store.py:21-29`) always calls `_ensure_file()`, which creates `.rush/`; `get_preference_readonly` exists (`:97`); `migrate_preference_store(project_root) -> int` (`migration.py:297-320`) writes rows one by one with `write_if_new` then renames, and has no `src/` caller; rows carry `expires_at` but no `scope`/`authority`/`applicability`; `GRANT_OPERATIONS` contains `overlay_read` and `resolve_memory_scope` checks the overlay owner (`authorization.py:1254-1260`). Interface per D10. New MemoryTool operation `preference` (count 22 → 23, D11) with actions `resolve`, `record`, `correct`, `end_task`, `migrate`; `migrate` resolves {list} with `apply=False` and {write} with `apply=True` in `policy` mode. Migration protocol per §2.2 D27 (receipt present → 0 rows, tombstone-aware skip, `E_MIGRATION` `reason="migration_verify_mismatch"` with no publication on mismatch, `os.link` publication to `.migrated.<sha16>`, never overwrite a differing backup). Applicability per D28; correction core per §2.1 item 4. Constructor creates nothing; `_ensure_file` runs only inside `set`, `delete`, `record`, `correct`, `end_task`, `migrate`.

### 72-T06 — Canonical public query and administration

Implement `72-T06 canonical memory query, correction and administration` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: All public surfaces share real scoped state and transaction results

All public surfaces share real scoped state and transaction results.


#### Required behavior

 Complete shared `MemoryTool` operations for all seven subjects. Extend existing `list`/`ask`/`expand`/`related`/`write`/`edit`/`archive`/`delete`/`promote`/`maintain`; add strict `history` and `explain` request variants, and use versioned `edit` plus `link` for correction/rejection/supersession. Empty-query `list` browses; nonempty `list` searches. Its request accepts subject(s), owner kind/ID, source, trust, freshness, archive mode and bounded cursor; result names exact `artifact_id`, `artifact_version`, `content_hash`, canonical project/worktree, provenance, current/unknown/stale reason, exclusion labels and next cursor. `history` takes exact ID and authorized historical mode; `explain` takes a cited selected ID or query and returns structured supporting/contrary/attempted records and unknowns, never generated rationale. Historical view requires explicit permission. Mutation requires exact selected owner, ID, expected version/revisions, reviewed `apply:true`, and existing grant; refusal/conflict leaves original bytes and user-entered form content. Repeated invocation/effect ID is idempotent; matching content alone does not dedupe independent writes. Promotion creates a new candidate and its decision version and preserves immutable original. Schema 2 result `raw` is an object with `schema_version`, `items` (each carries `reference` plus existing compatible identity fields), `next_cursor`, `complete`, `code` and `unknowns`; history/explain/import result variants have their named evidence/version/mapping fields and use same outer ToolResult. Correction reports affected relations/summaries/task guidance and invalidates future ordinary recall as applicable. Run through same `MemoryTool` for CLI/full MCP/dashboard/TUI; restricted memory-session MCP stays restricted. Retain ToolResult and existing operation compatibility; no browser-only rows or independent admin database.

#### Deliverables

#### Constraints

No duplicated permission/admin dispatcher, trust retagging, mixed strict/legacy forms, content-equality dedupe or restricted-profile administration.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow T01–T05 for exposed operations; optional hybrid does not block admin. Implement §2.1 request/result and transaction contracts in `src/rush/mcp_support/request_models.py` (Phase 70 dynamic `_memory_spec`, `_memory_tag`, `memory_request_model_keys`), tool dispatch/registry and store. Existing seven subjects are active_context, episodic, preference, failure, architectural_decision, domain_knowledge, skill_pattern; advanced repair/procedure operations remain content kinds, not undeclared subjects. History returns exact immutable versions with owner/source permission before content. For PAS import expose `TypedArtifactStore.apply_import_records` proposed seam through one existing SQLite transaction; capsule codec/import orchestration remains 73-T09.

#### Feature: Capsules carry authorized evidence offline and import as reviewable evidence

`rush_memory(operation="capsule")` and `rush memory capsule` export and import `rush_v1`/`pas_v1` capsules under the current grant, an approved mapping and the D18 import seam.

#### Required behavior

1. Request `rush_memory(operation="capsule", request={action, format, input, output, max_bytes, max_tokens, mapping_id, expected_digest, expected_store_revision, confirm_constraint_ids, apply, invocation_id, selectors})` with a strict model in `request_models.py`: unknown key (including `effect_id`), bool-as-int, wrong type → `E_INPUT`, zero effects. `invocation_id` is a UUID string: optional on `preview_export`/`preview_import` (generated when omitted and returned in the preview, inside the preview digest), required on `export`/`import` and equal to the preview's (a different value fails the item 2 `expected_digest` check); `capsule.py` computes `effect_id = sha256(compact.canonical_json([invocation_id, apply_action, canonical_target]))` (`delivery/compact.py:184`; `canonical_target` as Lock order position 4). `action` ∈ `preview_export|export|preview_import|import|bridge`; `format` ∈ `rush_v1|pas_v1`, default `rush_v1`. `bridge` → `E_INPUT` `reason="bridge_not_available"` (73-T03 owns it).
2. Presence: `preview_export`/`export` require `output`, reject `input`; `preview_import`/`import` require `input`, reject `output`; `import` requires `apply: true`; `apply` on any other action → `E_INPUT`; `confirm_constraint_ids` only on `preview_import`/`import`, default `[]`; optional strict `selectors` `{task_ref: {artifact_id: nonempty str, version: int ≥ 1}, owner_scope: {kind, id}, sources: [unique nonempty str], include_personal: bool (default false)}` (`owner_scope` per `OwnerScope.from_value`, `store.py:280`), every key optional; `task_ref`/`sources`/`include_personal` only on `preview_export`/`export`, `owner_scope` on every action except `bridge`; normalized `selectors` (sorted `sources`, omitted keys at defaults) are preview-digest fields; violation → `E_INPUT`. `export`/`import` require `expected_digest` equal to the preview digest; `import` also requires `expected_store_revision`; absent → `E_INPUT`; `expected_digest` mismatch → `E_VERSION` `reason="recovery_required"`; `expected_store_revision` mismatch → `E_VERSION` `reason="store_changed"` (D18).
3. Budgets: `max_bytes`/`max_tokens` strict int ≥ 1, default 65536/8192; a requested value above the hard cap 1048576/131072 → `E_INPUT` `reason="budget_above_hard_cap"` (invalid request value); content (an export or parsed input) whose required bytes or `retrieval._count_tokens(text, retrieval.DEFAULT_ENCODING)` exceed the hard cap or the request limits → `E_BUDGET` with `required: {bytes, tokens}` and unchanged scope before any write (Phase 73 plan line 419: "Above a hard cap, return `E_BUDGET`"); limits never rise automatically.
4. Existing codes only: malformed bytes, unknown schema, duplicate JSON key, digest or item-hash mismatch, PAS/sidecar pair mismatch → `E_INPUT` `reason="capsule_invalid"`; unredactable mandatory secret/private body → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="export_blocked"`, no output; mandatory content over budget → `E_BUDGET` `reason="export_blocked"`; changed capsule bytes, preview digest or policy revision since preview → `E_VERSION` `reason="recovery_required"`; store drift → `E_VERSION` `reason="store_changed"` (D18); grant or mapping denial → `E_PERMISSION` with the resolve reason.
5. Authority per D2: `preview_export`/`export` resolve {expand}; `preview_import` {list}; `import` {write}. Access resolves with `owner_scope = selectors.owner_scope` and `requested_sources = selectors.sources` (omitted owner with more than one granted owner → resolve `owner_required`, `authorization.py:1249` → `E_PERMISSION`). Export task: eligible rows are `evaluate_artifact_visibility`-usable current-version `episodic` rows with `content.kind == "checkpoint"`, `content.schema_version == 1`, `content.project_id`/`content.worktree_id` equal to the access identities; only the row with the largest `(created_at, id)` per `content.task_id` counts, and its task is eligible only when that row's `event_kind ∉ {task_end, task_cancel}`; `selectors.task_ref` names exactly one eligible row (absent or not usable → `E_NOT_VISIBLE`; version not current, or a usable checkpoint that is not its task's newest → `E_VERSION` `reason="recovery_required"`; the newest row of an ended or cancelled task → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="export_blocked"`); omitted with more than one eligible `task_id` → `E_INPUT` `reason="task_ref_required"`; zero eligible rows → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="export_blocked"`. Projection to the Phase 73 line 421 `task` object, keys exactly: `description`, `objective` ← same keys; `completed` ← `implemented`; `remaining` ← `unresolved`; `next_action` ← `next_action`; `constraints` ← `requirements` entries `{id: str, text: str, authority: str, provenance: str}` as `{constraint_id: id, text, origin_ref: provenance}`; `decisions` ← `decisions`; `failed_approaches` ← `disproved`; `blockers` ← `blockers`; `discoveries` ← `discoveries`; `last_verified_checkpoint` ← the last `checks` entry `{command: [str, …], exit_code: int, status: "passed" or "failed", source_revision: str, receipt_id: str}` with `status == "passed"` and `exit_code == 0`, exported as `{command, status, source_revision}`. Evidence ← exactly the row's `evidence_refs` `[{artifact_id, version}]`, each usable at that version under the export access, or under the overlay access (`user_overlay_owner_scope()`, D2 overlay `expand` {overlay_read, expand}) only when `selectors.include_personal` is true; a missing mandatory key, a value whose type differs from this item (`description`/`objective` str; `decisions`/`blockers`/`discoveries`/`disproved` entries `{text: str, origin_ref: str}`; `evidence_refs` entries `{artifact_id: str, version: int ≥ 1, not bool}`; `requirements`/`checks` entries as above), no passing `checks` entry, a non-usable or changed ref, or an overlay-owned ref without `include_personal` → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="export_blocked"`, no output. Row schema = 73-T04 checkpoint schema plus `description`, `objective`, `decisions`, `blockers`, `discoveries`, `evidence_refs` (Phase 73 line 201 amendment); CODEC only reads it, rows are written through the existing `write` operation, the 73-T04 producer stays Phase 73. Export runs secret/scope filtering before serialization and needs `--allow-artifact-write`; `import` needs `--allow-cache-write` (PR8); previews need no permission and create no store, lock, directory, output or policy file.
6. `rush_v1` file per Phase 73 plan line 421 (sorted-key UTF-8 JSON, `schema_version: 1`, `format: "rush_v1"`, `payload_sha256` over every other top-level field via `compact.canonical_json`); `pas_v1` reads/writes the PAS schema-1 session plus sidecar pair per Phase 73 plan lines 417-469, every unrepresentable field listed in the loss manifest; no PAS dependency.
7. Import: validate full bytes and every item hash, then `preview_import_records` (D18) and pass its `preview_binding` unchanged to `apply_import_records(records, preview_binding, expected_store_revision, expected_versions, confirm_foreign_ids, confirm_constraint_ids, invocation_id, effect_id, access)` (D18; Phase 73 plan line 449 states the same signature); `expected_store_revision` = preview `store_revision`; `confirm_foreign_ids` = the selected foreign-origin set; replay of the same `(invocation_id, effect_id)` and digest returns the original receipt.
8. Mapping: when capsule origin `(project_id, worktree_id)` ≠ `authorization.project_identity(root)`, `import`/`preview_import` need `mapping_id`; `resolve_capsule_mapping` rechecks the mapping and its grant on every import; the import access resolves with `owner_scope` = the mapping's `target_owner_scope` when a mapping is used (a different `selectors.owner_scope` → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="mapping_mismatch"`), else `selectors.owner_scope`; a capsule field or request flag never creates a mapping.
9. Locks per Lock order: policy lock (2) → `"memory-capsule-target:" + <canonical DB path for import | --output path for export>` (4) → `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` (5); never the dashboard project lock (Lock order rule "Capsule actions"). Export serializes `data` from objects already filtered by `SanitizedJsonValue.from_value` (`io/atomic_file.py:57`), then `s = SanitizedBytes.from_bytes(data)` (`:38`); `s.redaction_count != 0` or `s.data != data` → `E_PERMISSION` `reason="export_blocked"`, no output; else, after a final `access.recheck()`, `AtomicFile(PhysicalRoot(output.parent)).write_bytes(output.name, s)` (`:69`), so the written bytes equal `data`, the bytes the sanitizer checked. `pas_v1` export is a two-file staged export under the same target lock: both files staged and fsynced in the output directory, a journal records old/absent targets and intended digests, the PAS file replaces first and the sidecar `<output>.rush-manifest.json` replaces last (commit marker); a pre-commit failure restores only a partial replacement whose digest is unchanged; a concurrent edit → `E_VERSION` `reason="recovery_required"` with both target bytes kept (Phase 73 plan lines 427, 461).
10. CLI: `rush memory capsule` group; leaves `preview-export`, `export`, `preview-import`, `import`, `bridge`, each with `--project PATH` (default `.`), `--format`, `--input`, `--output`, `--max-bytes`, `--max-tokens`, `--mapping-id`, `--expected-digest`, `--expected-store-revision`, repeatable `--confirm-constraint-id`, `--invocation-id UUID`, `--task-ref ID:VERSION` (split on the last `:`), `--owner KIND:ID` (split on the first `:`), repeatable `--source`, `--include-personal`, `--apply`, `@permission_options`, `--json`, building the same request and calling `MemoryTool().run(root, operation="capsule", ...)`; JSON output equals MCP `raw`. Subgroup `rush memory capsule mapping`: `create --input FILE --project PATH [--owner KIND:ID] --expected-policy-revision N [--approve] @permission_options --json` and `revoke --mapping-id ID --expected-policy-revision N [--approve] @permission_options --json`; without `--approve` a read-only preview (origin pair, target pair, target owner, grant id, current revision); `--approve` without `--allow-cache-write` → `skipped` naming `cache_write` (`permissions.check_permissions`), zero writes (PR8); stale revision → `E_VERSION`, zero writes. No MCP, dashboard or TUI route creates or revokes a mapping.
11. Restricted `--memory-session` MCP profile: `capsule` → `E_PERMISSION`, DB digest unchanged.
12. Unchanged: every existing operation, the ToolResult envelope, `bridge` behavior beyond item 1, base64/utf8 expansion.

### 73-T02/BOOTSTRAP-CONSENT — Consent CLI checkpoint

Implement `73-T02/BOOTSTRAP-CONSENT grant, revoke, binding and denial guidance` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.2 D16, D19, D20, Lock order, `docs/phase-plans/phase-73-agent-activation-and-continuity-plan.md` lines 111-131 and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: A user grants, revokes and binds memory scope only through explicit local CLI consent

`rush memory grant`, `rush memory revoke` and `rush agent connect --memory-binding` are the only routes that create, revoke or bind a grant, and every policy denial names the command that fixes it.

#### Required behavior

1. `rush memory grant --project PATH (default .) [--owner KIND:ID | --overlay] (--source S ... | --all-current-sources) [--operation OP ...] [--expires-at EPOCH_SECONDS] --expected-policy-revision N [--approve] @permission_options [--json]`. `--owner` splits on the first `:` into `OwnerScope`; omitted → `legacy_owner_scope(root)` (`store.py:293`); `--overlay` → `user_overlay_owner_scope()` (`authorization.py:217`); `--owner` with `--overlay`, or `--source` with `--all-current-sources`, or neither source option → Click usage error exit 2. Omitted `--operation` → `ask,bootstrap,expand,history,list,write`, plus `overlay_read` with `--overlay` (all in `GRANT_OPERATIONS`, `authorization.py:48`; `grant_memory_scope` refuses `overlay_read` on a non-overlay owner, `:870-873`; resolve requires it for overlay reads, `:1254-1259`); an explicit `--operation` list is used exactly. `--expires-at` is epoch seconds (`float`), passed unchanged (PR9).
2. Without `--approve`: read-only preview from `TypedArtifactStore.open_readonly_view(root)` + new `consent_inventory()` (`SELECT owner_scope_kind, owner_scope_id, source, COUNT(*) FROM memory_artifacts WHERE archived_at IS NULL GROUP BY 1,2,3`): distinct source labels and owner scopes with row counts, resolved owner, operations, current `policy_revision` from `load_policy_readonly`, and the exact approve command; zero writes, locks or directories (absent store → empty inventory); `open_readonly_view` state `migration_required` → `E_MIGRATION` with the upgrade next action; `corrupt`/`busy` → the existing `readonly_state_code` code `E_STORE_CORRUPT`/`E_STORE_BUSY` (`store.py:229-234`) with `readonly_view_reason`; zero writes in every case.
3. With `--approve`: requires `--allow-cache-write` (`check_permissions`, `permissions.py:44`; PR8) else status `skipped` naming `cache_write`, zero writes; then `grant_memory_scope(project_root=..., owner_scope=..., approved_sources=..., allowed_operations=..., expected_policy_revision=N, consent=True, expires_at=...)` (keyword-only) (`authorization.py:843`); `--all-current-sources` is preview-only: the preview's exact approve command lists the inventory sources of the resolved owner as explicit `--source` flags, and `--approve` with `--all-current-sources` → Click usage error exit 2 (a source added after the preview is never granted); stale N on a new or changed grant → `E_VERSION`, no write; an identical active grant (same sources, operations and expiry) is returned before any revision check (`authorization.py:906-913`), no write; `MemoryPolicyError(code, reason)` → that code and reason.
4. `rush memory revoke --grant-id ID --expected-policy-revision N [--approve] @permission_options [--json]`: preview shows the grant view and its bindings, zero writes; approve (needs `--allow-cache-write`, PR8) calls `revoke_memory_scope(grant_id=ID, expected_policy_revision=N, consent=True)` (`:949`, keyword-only); stale N on an active grant → `E_VERSION`, no write; an already revoked grant returns the writer's idempotent `{grant_id, policy_revision, revoked: true, revoked_bindings}` before any revision check (`:971-981`), no write.
5. `rush agent connect AGENT --session S ... --memory-binding --memory-grant-id ID --expected-policy-revision N [--approve-memory-binding]`: `--memory-binding` requires the other two and `--project`, and the other two require it (usage error exit 2). Before any connect write, `load_policy_readonly` must show `policy_revision == N` and grant ID active, else `E_VERSION`/`E_PERMISSION`, and the grant view's `project_id`, `worktree_id`, `project_root` must equal `project_identity(project_path)`'s `project_id`, `worktree_id`, `canonical_root` (a `None` id never matches), else `E_PERMISSION` `reason="binding_scope_mismatch"` (resolve's reason, `authorization.py:1199`); then `resolve_memory_scope(project_root=project_path, operation=<the grant view's first allowed_operations entry>, owner_scope=<the grant view's owner_scope>)` must return `grant_id == ID`, else `E_PERMISSION` with the resolve reason (`principal_mismatch`, `relinked`, `worktree_mismatch`, `revoked`, `expired`), so every check `register_memory_binding` repeats after connect (`authorization.py:1027-1037`) is decided before connect; every refusal has zero connect, config or policy writes. Without `--approve-memory-binding`: connect runs unchanged and `raw["memory_binding"] = {"state": "preview", grant_id, session_id, host_id, expected_policy_revision}`. With it: after a successful connect, `register_memory_binding(grant_id=ID, session_id=S, host_id=AGENT, expected_policy_revision=N, consent=True)` (`:1006`) and `raw["memory_binding"]` = the binding view; a CAS conflict at that point → `raw["memory_binding"] = {"state": "conflict", "code": "E_VERSION"}`, connect result unchanged. Implemented in `cli.py` only: `AgentConnectionTool.run`, `connect_agent` and MCP `rush_agent_connection` (`mcp.py:919`) gain nothing.
6. `rush agent disconnect` (CLI and MCP, via `_disconnect`, `tools/agent_connection.py:678`) adds read-only `raw["memory"] = {"grants": [<grant view>], "bindings": [<binding view>]}`, computed after `disconnect_agent` returns from `load_policy_readonly(data_root=data_root)` (`_disconnect` `project_root`/`data_root` parameters): active grants whose `project_id`/`worktree_id` equal `project_identity(root)` and active bindings with `host_id == agent_id`; `project_root` None → `grants: []` and bindings with `host_id == agent_id` only; policy bytes unchanged; nothing revoked.
7. Every `E_PERMISSION` from a policy resolve on a §2.3 route carries `raw["next_action"] = memory_next_action(reason=..., project_root=..., operations=retrieval.required_operation_set(...) of the denied route (D2), owner_scope=<request owner_scope or None>, requested_sources=<request sources>)` via `tools/memory.py::_attach_policy_next_action`: `project_not_registered` → `{"text", "command": "rush project add <root> --allow-cache-write"}` (PR10); `policy_missing`, `no_grant`, `operation_not_granted`, `source_not_granted`, `owner_not_granted` → `{"text", "command": memory_grant_command(...)}`: a read-only-built preview `rush memory grant` command for that root with `--overlay` when the requested owner is `user_overlay_owner_scope()`, else `--owner KIND:ID` of the requested owner, else (no owner requested) `--owner KIND:ID` of the single non-overlay owner holding an active grant of the current principal on that project and worktree (the owner resolve stage 12 selects, `authorization.py:1236-1249`), else no `--owner` (`legacy_owner_scope(root)`); one `--operation` per member, in sorted order, of the required set ∪ the `allowed_operations` of the non-revoked grant of the current principal with the same project, worktree and owner (the grant `grant_memory_scope` supersedes, `authorization.py:901-923`); one `--source` per member, in sorted order, of the requested sources ∪ that grant's `approved_sources`, or `--all-current-sources` when both are empty; every command in items 2 and 7 (including `rush project add <root>`) is built as an argv list and rendered by `shlex.join` (POSIX) or `subprocess.list2cmdline` (`os.name == "nt"`), so the root, `KIND:ID`, every operation and every source are quoted; on every OS an argv element containing a character of Unicode category `Cc` (including `\n`, `\r`, ESC) or `Cf` (including U+202E) or `Cs` (a lone surrogate), as `authorization._require_label` refuses (`authorization.py:735`), and on Windows also any of `"%^&|<>()!$;` or a backtick, yields `command: None` with a `text` naming the refused value; `--expected-policy-revision` = `load_policy_readonly()["policy_revision"]`; other reasons → `{"text", "command": None}`. 72-T07 renders `raw.next_action.text` (D19).
8. No MCP, dashboard or TUI route creates, revokes or binds a grant; `bootstrap` operation, dispatcher and `tests/test_memory_activation_lifecycle.py` stay BOOTSTRAP-RUNTIME (Phase 73).

#### Deliverables

- `src/rush/cli.py`: `memory grant`, `memory revoke` leaves; `agent connect` options `--memory-binding`, `--memory-grant-id`, `--expected-policy-revision`, `--approve-memory-binding` and the binding step.
- `src/rush/memory/authorization.py`: `memory_grant_command(*, project_root: Path, operations: Sequence[str], owner_scope: OwnerScope | None = None, requested_sources: Sequence[str] = (), data_root: Path | None = None) -> str`, `memory_next_action(*, reason: str, project_root: Path, operations: Sequence[str], owner_scope: OwnerScope | None = None, requested_sources: Sequence[str] = (), data_root: Path | None = None) -> dict[str, str | None]` (reuses CODEC's `project_identity`).
- `src/rush/memory/store.py`: `TypedArtifactStore.consent_inventory(self) -> list[dict[str, Any]]` (read method, valid on `open_readonly_view`).
- `src/rush/tools/memory.py`: `_attach_policy_next_action`.
- `src/rush/tools/agent_connection.py`: `_disconnect` read-only listing.

### 72-T07 — Search, correction and explanation in CLI, TUI and dashboard

Implement `72-T07 complete user memory search, correction and explanation` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: CLI, keyboard TUI and browser expose same authorized evidence

CLI, keyboard TUI and browser expose same authorized evidence.


#### Required behavior

 CLI human and JSON output show subject, owner, source, trust, freshness, version, citation and next action; `rush memory` can browse without query and follow page/inspect/history/related/correction. TUI offers all seven subjects, owner and filter controls, list/selection/expansion/history/correction, preserved keyboard focus and entered content on conflict. Dashboard renders actual search/owner/archive/freshness filters, paginated selection, exact UTF-8 expansion, relation/evidence explanation and canonical mutation forms instead of JSON dump. For “why,” “what tried,” and “what stale,” show cited exact records, contrary evidence, alternatives, unknown gaps and affected code refs where real; absent rationale says unknown. Render hostile content as text. Each interface distinguishes absent store, empty, denied, stale, expired, revoked, partial, server error, and conflict, with a concrete retry/refresh/review action. No auto-approval or read-side initialization.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/cli.py` memory commands; `src/rush/cli_support/rendering.py`; `src/rush/tui.py::TuiState`, `_memory_refresh`, `_render_memory_admin`, `_handle_memory_key` and existing memory handlers; `src/rush/dashboard/server.py::_build_memory_section`, `_dispatch_memory_query` and existing memory action adapters; `src/rush/dashboard/application.js::renderMemorySection`, `switchSection` memory query path, `ACTION_FORMS` (`:515-573`) adding query, expand and maintain; `src/rush/dashboard/static_assets.py` for memory-panel styles (page security policy blocks injected styles); `tests/test_phase72_memory_ui.py::test_t07_shared_user_journey` and `::test_t07_dashboard_memory_panel_owner_version_lifecycle`; `tests/test_phase70_tui_usability.py::test_t28d_memory_workflows`; `tests/test_dashboard_memory_tokens.py` memory-specific cases. `panels.js` and `tests/test_phase71_dashboard_integration.py` are not created (D12). Use T06 API, not parallel frontend semantics.

#### Constraints

Reuse Phase 70 TUI controls/read-only adapters and current dashboard render path. No generic JSON as final UX, unsafe HTML, inaccessible form, synthetic status or reliance on unimplemented Phase 71 panel.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow T06, CODEC and BOOTSTRAP-CONSENT (§5.0 order); actual-use labels consume the existing Phase 70 T19 receipt readers (`read_artifact_receipts_readonly`, `telemetry.read_artifact_expansion_receipts_readonly`), with `unavailable` status where none exists. Current files are `application.js`/`server.py`/`static_assets.py`; `panels.js` is Phase 71's (D12). Add bounded accessible controls with associated labels, status/live-error region, stable focus after paging/conflict, escape/cancel, keyboard-only submit/selection, text-safe hostile content, desktop/mobile responsive layout and reduced-motion behavior. Current test_t28d journey is baseline, extend its real keyboard/backend assertions; separate `_MemoryRunSpy` layout checks cannot prove mutation persistence.

Concurrent edit keeps form text and emits version conflict; project switch during request discards foreign late response. Tab reaches search/filter/list/inspect/edit controls, Enter submits and Escape cancels with 0 writes; focus returns to selected record. Hostile `<img onerror=...>` visible as literal text and no event fires. Mobile 390×844 and desktop 1280×800 retain controls; reduced motion preserves progress state. Missing/denied/corrupt/empty/unknown/expired/revoked states have distinct reason and concrete action.

**RED → GREEN:** Run two real owners and all seven subjects through CLI, terminal keyboard and browser. Search/paginate, select exact version, expand/historical inspect, correct/reject/supersede, archive/restore/delete, deny grant, stale selected version, revoked source, hostile HTML and interrupted refresh. Compare displayed IDs/versions and persisted state to `MemoryTool`; verify no query falls to false empty or JSON-only output. Run `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_phase72_memory_ui.py tests/test_phase70_tui_usability.py -k 't07 or t28d' -q` and `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_dashboard_memory_tokens.py tests/test_phase70_t28d.py tests/test_phase70_t28d_backend.py tests/test_phase70_t27.py -q`. Complete actual PTY keyboard and browser desktop/mobile walkthrough on installed/current app; capture identities, actions, states and observed results in T09 report.

### 72-T08 — Evaluate optional hybrid retrieval

Implement `72-T08 scope-safe optional hybrid retrieval and honest paired evaluation` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: Optional retrieval earns benefit claim through matched real tasks

Optional retrieval earns benefit claim through matched real tasks.


#### Required behavior

 Reuse `retrieval.py::hybrid_candidates` (`:1925`)/`hybrid_page` (`:2078`, `_VECTOR_SCAN_CAP=512` at `:72`) and installed embedding configuration. Apply T01 eligibility before vector scoring; exact version/content hash invalidates stale embeddings (already keyed per §2 fact). No engine, network denial (`embedding_fallback_code=network_not_granted`, D14), invalid vectors or timeout returns labeled lexical fallback with precise reason, not hybrid success. On a `benefit` verdict with no unsafe/unavailable/harm verdict, hybrid becomes the default retrieval when a local embedding engine is configured (D14; configuration, verdict rule and metadata arm per D14a; cache fill and completeness per D39). Compare lexical, metadata and optional hybrid on predeclared held-out paraphrase/lexical-miss tasks using identical corpus, allowlist, grants, item/token/byte budget and relevance judgments. Record recall@k, false inclusion, unauthorized/stale inclusion, latency, full embedding/retrieval cost, trial counts, retries and uncertainty (C08); promotion of hybrid only by the D14 rule; no new mandatory model. A hybrid candidate cap or no cursor stays an explicitly labeled incomplete result until a real paged route proves completeness; never claim exhaustive retrieval from capped pool.

#### Deliverables

 `src/rush/memory/retrieval.py::hybrid_candidates`, `hybrid_page`; `src/rush/tools/memory.py` and `src/rush/mcp_support/request_models.py` v2 `retrieval`/embedding fields (D14); `tests/test_phase72_memory_hybrid.py::test_t08_scope_fallback_and_heldout_relevance`; `scripts/benchmarks/run.py` and `scripts/benchmarks/memory_datasets.py` defect fixes R8–R10; `scripts/benchmarks/memory.py` existing scenario harness only, after the 75-T02/EVALUATOR checkpoint; append exactly the 24 frozen `p72-` cases (72-T08 Frozen corpus; neighbor, denied-owner, revoked-source and stale-hash records are corpus roles, not cases) to `tests/fixtures/phase75/task_manifest.json` (created by EVALUATOR) without removing or changing its other required base families or variants; raw output under `docs/reports/phase-72-memory-eval/{current,hybrid,metadata}/` (D15) and summary in `docs/reports/phase-72-memory-acceptance.md`, each with G13 coverage receipts. No new benchmark framework; `store.py::put_embedding` gains the D39 version/content-hash revalidation; `catalog.py` declares the D14a options.

#### Constraints

No mandatory embeddings/provider/model, network without grant, synthetic relevance or promotion from capped retrieval. No invented successful benchmark route.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow T01/T02 and the 75-T02/EVALUATOR checkpoint (§5.0 row 10, D13). Declare dataset freeze before observations; task manifest adds Phase 72 `p72-` cases without replacing the 75-T02/EVALUATOR baseline. `--decision-mode`, `--decision-route`, `--task-manifest` are EVALUATOR deliverables; `--allow-live-route`, `--variant`, `--seeds`, `--output`, `--max-total-tokens` exist now. Before executing, `python -m scripts.benchmarks.run --help` must list every flag used, and `ollama list` must show `nomic-embed-text` (pull only after free disk ≥ 8 GiB plus model size). Missing route leaves benchmark evidence open; policy/unit implementation still completes.

### 72-T09 — Truthful docs and live acceptance

Implement `72-T09 truthful documentation and frozen live acceptance` in `rush-cli` in the current working directory.

Read `AGENTS.md`, this plan §2.1, `docs/phase-plans/memory-program-contract.md` and `docs/user-guide/working-with-ai-agents.md` first; they are binding.

#### Feature: Every published claim names route, revision and actual observed evidence

Every published claim names route, revision and actual observed evidence.


#### Required behavior

 Reconcile architecture proposal, Phase 63/MC13 historical claims, current API/transport/client matrix and this phase's actual implemented/evaluated routes. Mark each claim proposed, implemented, integrated, evaluated, or live-checked with revision. Preserve old receipts and explain superseded verdicts; do not rewrite history into success. Document user goal, minimum consent/input, all-subject search and correction, visible authority/provenance, denied/stale/recovery states, exact CLI/MCP/TUI/dashboard examples only after executing them. Freeze source bytes before final verification. Real acceptance uses two permitted agents on same project and two projects with consented personal overlay; one agent writes/corrects, another recalls exact authorized revision, acts on it and identifies stale/denied evidence. E06/E25 live cases include held-out rewording of an explicitly rejected approach, an unrelated lexical lookalike and later explicit reversal: second agent selects allowed alternative only in affected scope, cites rejection ref, permits unrelated case and honors reversal. Retrieval hits alone do not close that behavior. Test actual browser and PTY states; existing Phase 70/71 broader acceptance remains separately open unless its full contract passes.

#### Deliverables

 `docs/reports/phase-72-memory-acceptance.md` with source hash, commands, test results, platform/client versions, consent/grants, redacted IDs/versions, screenshot/terminal observation references only if captured, failures and exact blockers; user/documentation paths in §3; `tests/test_phase72_memory_acceptance.py::test_t09_integrated_route_and_scope` for local integration. No agent-secret content in receipts.

#### Constraints

No product readiness from plan bytes/unit suite, no erased historical receipts, unavailable native route counted as pass or unsupported installed version silently tested.

**Ordered implementation and dependencies:** Follow all owned implemented routes including §8 PAS. Freeze source tree digests, build/installed executable hash and versions before tests. Resolve source versus installed parity explicitly; changed dirty Phase 70 bytes invalidate affected observations. Record platform/client grants/commands/results/errors and independent plan-vs-code check. Run current supported macOS/Linux/Windows native lanes demanded by consumed Phase 70/71 contracts; absent runner stays exact external blocker, not closed. Capture screenshot evidence only if separately requested; text browser/PTY receipts are sufficient when capture unrequested.

#### Checks to run before reporting

All of these must pass: the task's tests pass with zero skips; the commands in this section, then §5.0 G11, where the full suite fails only on the two G16 task-start baseline tests.

**Concrete fixture and exact acceptance:** Two agents A/B in same authorized project: A writes r1@1, B receives exact version 1 and acts on it; A corrects to version 2, B's subsequent read returns version 2 and rejects cached version 1. Two projects share only consented personal key; each project's private row absent from other. Actual stdio MCP init/tools/call, installed CLI, PTY and desktop/mobile browser use same IDs/versions; revoked grant blocks next use. PAS forged verification remains imported/pending until local applicability review; confirming intended constraint never confirms source test success. Full test/ruff gates preserve native Phase 70/71 open results and report one exact blocker per unavailable lane.

**Checks:** Run T01–T08 affected commands on frozen bytes, then `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python --version` (must print 3.12), `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -q -m ''`, `rtk proxy .venv/bin/ruff check src tests scripts`, and `rtk proxy .venv/bin/ruff format --check src tests scripts`. Also run `rtk proxy .venv/bin/mypy src/rush`, `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python scripts/sync_docs.py --check`, and `rtk git diff --check`; preserve exact failed checks as blockers rather than substituting a narrower command. Run installed CLI/MCP, PTY, browser desktop/mobile and personal-overlay cases named above. Review plan ledger against actual files and AST calls before accepting green tests. Do not repeat unchanged passing checks without a concrete failure or drift.

## 6. Failure, recovery, risks, and stop conditions

Read errors never initialize a missing DB, silently retry with broader scope, or substitute a stale/currently excluded item. Cursor drift returns restart with filters preserved; version conflict retains entered edits for review; tampered evidence is quarantined from ordinary use while historical inspection remains permissioned; unavailable embedding engine returns labeled lexical result; failed migration retains original file and exposes exact retry. Revocation blocks subsequent pages and direct expansion. A denied personal overlay remains off without changing project records. Maintenance row error reports exact affected ID without promoting from partial evidence.

Stop an acceptance claim on unauthorized or cross-owner exposure, stale/tampered `STATED` output, lost page, incorrect UTF-8/digest reconstruction, hidden write, changed source bytes during verification, or a UI claim without actual browser/terminal evidence. Repair offending route, refreeze and rerun affected checks. External provider/host availability blocks only its named lane. Do not turn unavailable into passed, delete user memory for recovery, or weaken permissions to make a demo work.

Main risk is divergent policy across old/new reads. T01 makes eligibility shared and T03/T06 reuse it. Second risk is authority confusion across personal/project/task layers; T05 records source and correction lineage, T07 shows it. Third is apparent retrieval benefit from unfair baseline or partial pool; T08 uses held-out matched tasks and reports costs/truncation. Concurrent plan edits to `store.py`, `memory.py` or `cli.py` require one integration writer and rerun of affected contract tests.

## 7. Reconciliation with existing work and readiness

Phase 70 T18/T20 supply useful-memory projection and permission/result semantics. T28-D's full terminal memory administration is implemented by T06 canonical operations plus T07 terminal route; its broader T28-F/Phase 70 gates are not implied by this plan's narrower checks. Phase 71 P07 owns the dashboard memory UX and live browser acceptance; T07 performs that exact memory work against T06, retaining its owner/version, promote, hostile-content and reload checks. AP07 activation/connection status belongs to Phase 73; this phase displays its real receipt producer once present and shows unavailable until then. AP08 is distributed: T09 verifies this phase's whole route, while other phases verify their own host/learning/token claims. F23 is coordinated here; each other phase documents only its actual new claims.

Execution resumes at §5.0 order rows 1–3 (72-T01/T02/T03 remaining work) on the Phase 72 branch. Release/readiness requires all T01–T09 behavior and owned live evidence; plan authorship itself changes no product behavior. Cross-phase whole-system confirmation is recorded by participating concluding tasks under shared C08/C09, not delegated to an unspecified later phase.

## 8. PAS integration: imported evidence and user control

User-requested addition, 2026-09-25, implementing recommendations PAS01/PAS04 from the [PAS comparison](../reports/pas-agent-portable-memory-comparison-2026-09-25.md). This section extends T01/T06/T07/T09; it removes none of their original requirements. Phase 73-T09 owns the portable format, codec, export/import orchestration and managed bridge. This phase owns canonical eligibility, mutation and user review; no duplicate importer or permission store.

**Required behavior:** Every Rush/PAS import is external evidence. The current local grant determines destination owner, project/worktree and permitted operation; source owner, author, timestamps, asserted completion and claimed trust remain provenance only. Imported explicit constraints/decisions appear as pending applicability until the current user confirms them through existing review/promotion semantics. An imported `verified`, `completed` or `STATED` assertion never grants local verified authority. Source anchors are unknown until validated against exact local content/revision; matching paths, branch names or a digest supplied by the same external file are insufficient. Confirming a constraint does not also certify its source's test outcomes.

`preview_import` performs read-only schema/hash/containment/policy validation and returns exact proposed records, destination mapping, collisions, redactions, unresolved source anchors and required consent. It creates no database, lock, key or journal. Bind preview to capsule bytes/digest, destination root/worktree, current policy revision, selected record set and relevant existing artifact versions. `import` with explicit apply uses that binding, rechecks current grant and source bytes, and applies one atomic version-checked transaction through the canonical store. Drift returns conflict with zero partial writes; keep preview/user choices for refresh. Repeated invocation/effect ID is idempotent; a distinct import never silently replaces an existing local constraint or evidence version. Preserve foreign origin IDs in provenance and use separate local artifact IDs. Corrections and contradictions remain inspectable instead of last-writer-wins merging.

Export applies the same owner/source/subject/visibility policy before selecting records. User-wide preferences require their existing separate overlay grant and explicit inclusion in this export; there is no blanket personal-memory export. Grants, capability tokens and credentials never enter a capsule or managed block. Exported selections remain unchanged in canonical storage. If redaction removes mandatory continuation evidence, report an incomplete/blocked export with exact safe reason; do not claim a self-contained continuation or mutate the original to make export pass. An explicitly redacted derivative gets its own digest and redaction manifest, never the original content hash. Historical, expired, rejected or revoked artifacts retain T01's restrictions.

**Deliverables/allowed files:** Extend T01/T06's existing `src/rush/memory/store.py`, `src/rush/tools/memory.py` policy/transaction seams and T07's `src/rush/cli_support/rendering.py`, `src/rush/tui.py`, `src/rush/dashboard/server.py`, `src/rush/dashboard/application.js` import-review projections (no `panels.js`, D12). Phase 73 owns `src/rush/memory/capsule.py` and capsule transport/CLI request definitions; changes to shared `memory.py` are serialized under C09. Extend `tests/test_phase72_portable_memory_policy.py` and `tests/test_memory_public_contract.py`; document authority and conflict handling in T09's existing user guides and `docs/reports/phase-72-memory-acceptance.md` only after the routes work.

**Constraints:** No imported instruction hierarchy, hidden global import, read-side setup, inferred consent, grant widening, independent import database or automatic promotion. User-facing imported-state labels distinguish claimed progress, locally verified progress, pending constraint, stale anchor and unavailable original. Imported source content renders as text. Preview/list/history remain usable when apply is denied.

**Checks:** `rtk proxy env -u PYTHONPATH .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_phase72_portable_memory_policy.py tests/test_memory_public_contract.py -q`. Required cases are `test_foreign_owner_and_verified_claim_do_not_authorize`, `test_imported_constraint_needs_local_applicability_review`, `test_preview_no_files_then_atomic_apply`, `test_changed_capsule_policy_and_artifact_version_conflict`, `test_replay_and_distinct_import_identity`, `test_export_personal_scope_and_required_secret_redaction`, and `test_import_review_cli_tui_dashboard_same_state`. Assert exact local owner/trust/freshness, unchanged prior versions, transaction rollback and persisted receipt identity. Use fresh stores, two owners, moved worktrees, contradictory constraints and concurrent edits. T09 live walkthrough imports one PAS session and one Rush capsule through CLI and app review, rejects a forged verification claim, confirms only an intended constraint and preserves the unrelated local choice.

**Atomic import seam:** T06 adds `TypedArtifactStore.apply_import_records(*, records, preview_binding, expected_store_revision: int, expected_versions, confirm_foreign_ids: tuple[str, ...], confirm_constraint_ids: tuple[str, ...], invocation_id, effect_id, access)` with the mandatory binding, ordering and absent-store publication of §2.2 D18; 73-T09 codec converts capsule entries to the D18 record schema without SQL; the store revalidates every record (D18). `preview_binding` contains capsule digest/selection/destination/policy revision/existing-version map, and `expected_versions` covers every colliding local ID. Under policy lock then one SQLite transaction recheck binding, allocate local IDs distinct from foreign IDs, sanitize content, insert imported/pending versions/provenance/receipt, and commit all or nothing. Persist capsule origin `(capsule_digest, foreign_origin_id)` plus effect request digest in existing receipt/version storage: replay returns original mapped IDs; differing bytes under same effect conflicts. Separate import retains local choices and exposes unresolved collision instead of replacing them. Crash before commit leaves 0 rows/receipt, crash after commit recovers same mapping. Export policy is checked immediately before emission, and receiver validation never restores source permissions.

**State at `fe1ffb0` and decisions:** `tests/test_phase72_portable_memory_policy.py` holds 1 of the 7 required cases (`test_foreign_owner_and_verified_claim_do_not_authorize`, `:95`); the other 6 and `apply_import_records`, `preview_import_records`, `find_import_effect_readonly` are absent; `capsule.py` is absent. D18 fixes import authority, grant operation and seam amendments. The CODEC checkpoint (§5.0 row 7) adds MemoryTool `capsule` (count 25 → 26, D11) and `authorization.py` capsule-mapping helpers `approve_capsule_mapping`, `revoke_capsule_mapping`, `resolve_capsule_mapping` with strict `purpose=capsule_import` records, CLI-only `rush memory capsule mapping create|revoke` taking `--approve` (with `--allow-cache-write`) plus `--expected-policy-revision` (CODEC item 10); `max_bytes`/`max_tokens` strict ints ≥1 default 65536/8192, requested values above 1048576/131072 → `E_INPUT`, payload over the hard cap or the request limits → `E_BUDGET` before effects; existing error codes only: `E_INPUT` `reason="capsule_invalid"`, `E_PERMISSION` or `E_BUDGET` `reason="export_blocked"`, `E_VERSION` `reason="recovery_required"` (CODEC item 4); lock id `memory-capsule-target:<path>`; the `bridge` action stays with 73-T03; capsule task records amend the 73-T04 schema; wire-contract selectors required only when ambiguous (owner decisions 2026-10-05).

**Completion and dependencies:** T01's policy and T06's transaction behavior can be developed on canonical record fixtures independently. The implementation consumes 73-T09's CODEC checkpoint (codec/public operation) and 73-T02's existing grant API; real portable-route closure also needs 73-T09's final release (75-T01 receipts, 75-T03/T04 budget and recovery), which stays in Phase 73, so the §8 live lane stays open with that exact blocker until it lands. T07/T09 must demonstrate searchable imported provenance and user review on that route. These acceptance cases are additional to T01–T09, not a substitute for their existing gates.
